Lines 7–15 govern November 15 (marked 'є҃і' in the right margin): The Holy Confessors Gurias, Samonas, and Habib ('Святых исповѣдник Гурия, Самона и Авива'): Vespers stichera on 6, Glory Tone 2 ('Едеса веселит[ся]...'), Aposticha Glory Tone 2 ('Приидѣте, мучениколюбцы...'), and lines 11–15 provide their complete Dismissal Troparion, Tone 5 ('Чудеса святых мученик град неразрушим нам подав, Христе Боже: молитвами их совѣты языком разори, и царствия скипетр утверди, яко един Благ и Человѣколюбець :~'). Lines 15–19 detail Matins (canons of Octoechos and saints on 6) and open their Kontakion after Ode 3, Tone 2 ('С высоты, мудрии, благодать вземше...'), continuing onto folio 118v.

[^274]: Folio 118v (November 15 & Weekday Fast Entrance Order): Lines 1–2 conclude the Ode 3 Kontakion of SS. Gurias, Samonas, and Habib, Tone 2 ('...избависте: вы бо есте воистину Едесу слава и миру радость :~'). Lines 2–7 provide their second Kontakion after Ode 6, Tone 2, Podoben: "Firm [and God-bearing]" (Твердыя): 'Избранныя и богоносныя мученики, и благочестия церковныя таинники похвалим, поюще Христа: Гурия, Самона, Авива, стечение Христа Бога нашего, Едесу слава и миру радость :~'. Lines 7–11 detail Liturgy: prokeimenon LXX Ps. 15:3, 8, Epistle 27th Sunday, Alleluia LXX Ps. 33:18, Gospel from John, and communion verse LXX Ps. 32:1. Lines 11–19 provide the detailed rubrics for beginning the Nativity Fast on a weekday (Monday through Friday): no prostrations at the beginning of Vespers, 14 stichera at 'Lord, I have cried' (combining Octoechos and saints), Alleluia in place of the prokeimenon, 'Vouchsafe, O Lord' with 3 prostrations, Octoechos Aposticha, 'Now lettest Thou depart', and 3 prostrations after the Trisagion, continuing onto folio 119r.

[^275]: Folio 119r (marked '119' in pencil at the top right; Nativity Fast Weekday Compline & Midnight Office): Lines 1–7 complete the Lenten Vespers order with the dismissal troparia with prostrations ('Богородице Дѣво...', 'Крестителю Христов...', 'Цѣлителю...', 'Под Твою милость...' without prostration), Lord have mercy (40), prayer of St. Ephrem with 3 great prostrations ('Господи и Владыко живота моего'), and dismissal. Lines 7–15 detail Great Compline for the fast: two choirs from 'When I cried', troparia 'Enlighten mine eyes...', supplicatory canon to the Theotokos, 40 Lord have mercy, prayer 'O undefiled, pure...', 15 great prostrations, and the prayer of St. Ephrem. Lines 15–19 record that this full order applies strictly to the first day of the fast ('Сий устав в первый день токмо'), whereas subsequent fast days follow simpler compline with prostrations like the Apostles' Fast ('якоже и в пост святых апостол, без пѣния просто'), transitioning to Midnight Office on folio 119v.

[^276]: Folio 119v (marked 'ѕ҃і' in the left margin; November 15/16): Lines 1–3 conclude the Nativity Fast weekday monastic office with Midnight Office and Matins prostrations following the Great Fast typikon ('по уставу Великаго поста'), ending with 15 great prostrations. Lines 3–10 open November 16 (marked 'ѕ҃і'): The Holy Apostle and Evangelist Matthew ('Святаго апостола Матѳеа евангелиста, празднуем весь день'), detailing Vespers stichera on 6, Glory Tone 4 ('Возвавшу тя...'), Aposticha Glory Tone 6 ('От дѣл злых...'), and lines 7–10 provide his complete Dismissal Troparion, Tone 2 ('Мытницю оставив, Евангелие прием, от тлѣнных небрегл еси, апостол бысть Христа Бога; Того моли непрестанно за душа наша :~'). Lines 10–18 govern Matins (canons of Octoechos and saint on 6) and give his complete Kontakion after Ode 6, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесыйся): 'Мытарьское иго отверг, и праведному игу сочетася, и явися купець предобрый, богатство восприем свыше премудрости; тѣмже проповѣдал еси истинное слово, и лѣнивых воздвигл еси душа, написав час судный :~'. Lines 18–19 detail Aposticha with verse LXX Ps. 18:5 and Idiomelon Tone 8 ('Воскликнем...'), running over to folio 120r.

[^277]: Folio 120r (marked '120' in pencil at the top right, gathering signature 'ма҃' at the bottom right; November 16 & 17): Lines 1–5 conclude Apostle Matthew: Aposticha Glory Tone 2, and Liturgy readings: prokeimenon LXX Ps. 18:5, 2, Epistle 1 Cor. 4:9–16 [зачало 10], Alleluia LXX Ps. 88:6, Gospel Mt. 9:9–13, and communion verse LXX Ps. 18:5. Lines 5–13 open November 17 (marked 'з҃і' in the right margin): Our Father among the Saints Gregory the Wonderworker, Bishop of Neocaesarea ('Иже во святых отца нашего Григория Чудотворца'): Vespers stichera on 6, Glory Tone 6 ('Глубину богословия...'), Aposticha Glory Tone 6 ('Скорость Божия...'), and lines 9–13 give his complete Dismissal Troparion, Tone 8 ('В молитвах бодр, чудес дѣянием прилежа, тезоименитна стяжа исправления; но молися Христу Богу, отче Григорие, просвѣтити душа наша, да некогда уснем в смерть :~'). Lines 13–19 govern Matins (canons of Octoechos and saint on 6) and provide his complete Kontakion after Ode 6, Tone 2 ('Чудес многих прием дѣйство, знаменьи страшными бѣсы устрашил еси, недуги огнал еси от человѣк, премудре Григорие; чудотворець же именуешися, званье от дѣл прием :~'), with Aposticha continuing onto folio 120v.

[^278]: Folio 120v (November 17, 18, 19 & 20): Lines 1–5 conclude St. Gregory Thaumaturgus: Aposticha verse LXX Ps. 115:6, Idiomelon Tone 4 ('Священства...'), and Liturgy readings: prokeimenon LXX Ps. 115:6, 3, Epistle Sunday after Theophany, Alleluia LXX Ps. 131:9, Gospel Mt. 4:23–5:13, and communion verse LXX Ps. 111:6. Lines 6–12 govern November 18 (marked 'и҃і' in the left margin): The Holy Martyrs Plato and Roman ('Святых мученик Платона и Романа'): Alleluia, troparion ('Мученицы Твои...'), and their complete Kontakion, Tone 3 ('Святая твоя память вселенную веселит, созывающи вся в честный храм твой, идеже нынѣ радостию веселится, поем в пѣснех и свѣтлостех; сего ради, Платоне, вопием: язычнаго нашествия избави град твой, святый :~'). Lines 13–14 record November 19 (marked 'ѳ҃і' in the left margin): The Holy Prophet Obadiah, and the Holy Martyr Barlaam ('Святаго пророка Авдия, и святаго мученика Варлаама, Аллилуиа'). Lines 14–19 open November 20 (marked 'к҃' in the left margin): Forefeast of the Entry of the Theotokos, and our Venerable Father Gregory Decapolite, and St. Proclus, Patriarch of Constantinople ('Предпразденство Входа Пресвятыя Богородицы, и преподобнаго отца Григория Декаполита, иже во святых отца Прокла, патриарха Царяграда'), detailing Vespers stichera (3 of Theotokos and 3 of Gregory), Glory and Both now Tone 4 ('Днесь свѣтовведѣтельница...'), continuing onto folio 121r.

[^279]: Folio 121r (marked '121' in pencil at the top right; November 20 & 21): Lines 1–10 conclude the Forefeast office of November 20: Aposticha Glory and Both now Tone 4 ('Приидѣте, вси людие...'), Troparion of the saints Tone 4 ('Боже отец наших...'), and lines 4–10 provide the Forefeast Troparion of the Theotokos, Tone 4 ('Радость обручает вселеннѣй нынѣ печали премѣнение, плод прозябши, едину Приснодѣву, еюже убо приводит молитвы исполняюще, днесь радующеся, церкви Господни, яко воистину церковь Божия Слова и Мати Чистая :~'). Lines 10–16 govern Matins: Forefeast canon on 6 and saints on 8, Kontakion of the Forefeast after Ode 6, Tone 4 ('Веселия днесь исполнися вселенная вся в нарочитом праздникѣ Богородицы, зовуще: Ты еси храм небесный :~'), and Aposticha Glory and Both now Tone 1 ('Возвеселися...'). Lines 17–19 open the Great Feast of November 21 (marked 'ка҃' in the right margin): The Entry into the Temple of our Most Holy Lady the Theotokos ('Вход в церковь Пресвятыя Владычица нашея Богородицы, празднуем весь день пѣнием всенощное, и малая вечерня'), running over to folio 121v.

[^280]: Folio 121v (November 21 — Entry of the Most Holy Theotokos into the Temple): Lines 1–6 complete the Small Vespers order opened on folio 121r: festal stichera on 4 ('Ѡ дивеное чюдо...'), Aposticha Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" ('Доме єфрантовь') with psalm verses LXX Ps. 44:15, 16. Lines 6–11 provide the complete Dismissal Troparion of the Entry, Tone 4 ('Дн҃ь благоволенїа бж҃їа проѡбраженїа · и чл҃ча сп҃сенїа проповѣданїа · въ цр҃кве бж҃ьнїа сно дв҃а авлѧетсѧ · и х҃а вьсѣмь бл҃говѣстоуеть · того и мы велегласно възпїемь · радоуижесѧ смотренїа ꙁижителева исплъненїа :~'). Lines 11–19 govern Great Vespers: 'Blessed is the man', stichera on 8 (Tone 1 'Ѡ дивеное чюдо' 4 times, Tone 4 'И ꙗко добла' 4 times), Glory... Both now... Tone 8 ('По роженїи твоемь...'), entrance, 3 Paremias, Litiya idiomela with Glory... Both now... Tone 2 ('Въси земнороднїи...'), Aposticha Tone 5, Glory... Both now... Tone 6 ('Дн҃ьс събор[и]...'), and blessing of the loaves (artoklasia).

[^281]: Folio 122r (marked '122' in pencil at the top right; November 21 — Entry of the Theotokos): Lines 1–8 govern Matins: Troparion of the feast thrice ('Дн҃ь бл҃говоленіе...'), reading of the patristic festal homily, customary Kathisma psalmody, Sessional hymns, Songs of Ascents (Anabathmoi) of Tone 4, prokeimenon LXX Ps. 44:11, 2, Gospel from Luke [Lk. 1:39–49, 56], Psalm 50, and two canons (Tone 4 by George the Hymnographer and Tone 1) with Katavasia 'Christ is born' sung by both choirs together. Lines 8–13 provide the complete Kontakion of the Entry after Ode 6, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесыйся): 'Пр҃чтаа цр҃кве нб҃сова · многоцѣнныи чертогъ дв҃аа · сщ҃енноекровище славы бж҃ьа · дн҃ь воводитсѧ в о домь · бл҃гть въвъдаще иже въ дс҃ѣ бж҃твенѣ · юже поють аг҃гли бж҃їи · та бо есть сѣнь нб҃наа :~'. Lines 13–17 detail Ode 9 (omitting the Magnificat/More honorable), Praises stichera on 4, Great Doxology, Dismissal Troparion, litany, dismissal, and distribution of holy unction to the brethren. Lines 18–19 outline Liturgy readings: canon Odes 3 and 6, kontakion, and Ode 9 festal refrains ('Величи душа моꙗ...').

[^282]: Folio 122v (November 21 & 22): Lines 1–7 complete the Liturgy of the Entry: prokeimenon verse LXX Ps. 44 / Lk. 1:48, Epistle to the Hebrews [Heb. 9:1–7], Alleluia LXX Ps. 44:11, 15, Gospel from Luke [Lk. 10:38–42, 11:27–28], communion verse LXX Ps. 115:4, refectory meal with fish and wine, and statutory notice that the feast lasts 5 days with its Apodosis on November 25 ('Вѣсто же есть · ꙗко празнуетсѧ настоѧщїи сеи праꙁникъ · е҃ · днїи · ѡдаетъжесѧ · къ · ке · день :~'). Lines 8–15 open November 22 (marked 'кв҃' in the left margin): The Holy Apostles Philemon, Archippus, Onesimus, and Apphia ('Ст҃го ап҃ла · Филимона · и иже с нимь'), directing that Kathisma psalmody is omitted at Vespers due to the vigil labor, with stichera (3 of feast, 3 of saint), Dismissal Troparion ('Ап҃лне ст҃и...'), and Matins canons (feast on 8, saint on 4). Lines 15–19 provide their complete Kontakion after Ode 3, Tone 2 ('Ꙗко звѣзды всесвѣтлыꙗ · просвѣщающаꙗ конца · ап҃лї хв҃и восхвали · Филимона славнаго · и Архипа сщ҃енаго · и Ѡнисима · с ними и Агафїю всемоудрую · въпїюще молите непрестанно ѡ всѣхъ насъ :~'), concluding on folio 123r.

[^283]: Folio 123r (marked '123' in pencil at the top right; November 22 & 23): Lines 1–3 conclude the office of Apostle Philemon with Kontakion of the Entry after Ode 6 and Liturgy readings of the day. Lines 4–9 open November 23 (marked 'к҃г' in the right margin): Our Father among the Saints Amphilochius, Bishop of Iconium, and St. Gregory, Bishop of Agrigentum ('Иже въ ст҃ыхъ ѡца нашего · Амфилохїа · єп҃па · Иконїискаго · и ст҃го ѡца Григорїа · єп҃па Акраганскаго'): Vespers stichera of the saints on 6, Troparion 'O God of our fathers...', and Matins canons. Lines 9–15 provide the complete Kontakion of St. Gregory of Agrigentum after Ode 3, Tone 8 ('Ꙗко добродѣтелемь положьника · и ст҃ымь оудобренїе · цр҃къви славить въпиюще · въспѣваетъ Григорье · молитвами твоими дароуи чьтоущимь въсегда троуды твоꙗ и брани · ѡбѣдь · и напастеи , избавленїе подаваꙗ · ꙗко беꙁь скврънень :~'). Lines 15–19 provide the Kontakion of St. Amphilochius of Iconium after Ode 6, Tone 2 ('Бж҃твьныи громь и троуба дх҃внаа · вѣрнымъ насадителю · и сѣкира на єретикы · ст҃лю Амфилохїе · тр҃цноу оугодниче , съ аг҃глы неизреченно молися непрестано ѡ всѣхъ насъ :~'), concluding on folio 123v.

[^284]: Folio 123v (November 23 & 24): Lines 1–5 conclude the Liturgy for SS. Amphilochius and Gregory: canon Odes 3 and 6, prokeimenon LXX Ps. 115:6, 3, Alleluia LXX Ps. 131:9, and Gospel Mt. 5:14–19. Lines 5–11 open November 24 (marked 'к҃д' in the left margin): The Holy Great-Martyrs Catherine of Alexandria and Mercurius of Caesarea ('В тъ жь памѧть · ст҃ыꙗ мч҃нцн · Екатерїны · и ст҃го мч҃нка Меркоурїа'): Vespers stichera on 6, Glory Tone 2 ('Радостно...'), Dismissal Troparion ('Моученницї твои г҃и...'), and Matins canons. Lines 11–15 give the complete Kontakion of St. Catherine after Ode 3, Tone 2 ('Ликъ чт҃ныи не бж҃твнѣи дв҃чнї колюбцемъ съставиша · чтоуще мд҃ру Екатерїну · та бо на соудищи х҃а проповѣда · и змиꙗ попра · и ритьѡрь разумы посрами :~'). Lines 15–19 open the Kontakion of St. Mercurius after Ode 6, Tone 2 ('Вь ꙁбранїе непобѣдимаго воина · и въ бѣдахъ непобѣдимаго застоупника · пѣнми Меркоурїꙗ прослави · празднующїи любовію пам[ять]...'), running over to folio 124r.

[^285]: Folio 124r (marked '124' in pencil at the top right; November 24 & 25): Lines 1–6 conclude the Kontakion of St. Mercurius ('...весело ѡ б҃ѣ избавляеть · и ѡ[т] скръбьн[и] :~') and Liturgy readings for the two martyrs: prokeimenon LXX Ps. 67:36, 27, Alleluia LXX Ps. 33:18, and Gospel from Luke. Lines 6–18 open November 25 (marked 'к҃є' in the right margin): Our Father among the Saints Clement, Pope of Rome, and Peter, Archbishop of Alexandria, upon which day occurs the Apodosis of the Entry of the Theotokos ('въ тъ дн҃ь ѡдаетсѧ праздникь · ст҃ыꙗ бц҃а'): Vespers combining 3 festal stichera ('Ѡ дивеное чюдо') and 3 to St. Clement, Glory... Both now... ('По роженїи твоемь...'), Aposticha with Glory Tone 4 to St. Peter ('Рѫкою бж҃їею...'), and Both now... to the feast ('Дн҃ьскорн...'), troparion 'O God of our fathers...', and Matins canons (feast on 8, saints two canons on 6). Lines 18–19 open the Kontakion of St. Clement after Ode 3, Tone 2 ('Цръковныꙗ тверди...'), continuing onto folio 124v.

[^286]: Folio 124v (November 25 — Apodosis of the Entry of the Theotokos): Lines 1–4 conclude the Ode 3 Kontakion of St. Clement of Rome, Tone 2 ('...ди бж҃твьнїи нб҃ове · благозаконьꙗ прѣдїжрїи · стлъпнеже шекрѣпцїи · Клименте премѫдре · съ бж҃твеноумь Петро, мольбами своими съхранита въсеꙗны :~'). Lines 4–10 provide the complete Kontakion of St. Peter of Alexandria after Ode 3, Tone 3 ('Православными повеленїми · цр҃ковь просвѣтивь, ꙁа нѧже пострадавь бг҃обл҃жене ѡч҃е · Арьꙗ ѡстѫпника ѡгнавь · тѣм ти прѣчистѫꙗ памѧть съвръшающе · православно въпїемь · радоуисѧ Петре камень вѣры :~'). Lines 10–15 detail Matins conclusion: Sessional hymns, Kontakion of the Entry after Ode 6, Praises stichera on 4, Glory and Both now Tone 2 ('Дн҃ь въ цр҃ковь въводитсѧ...'), Great Doxology, and Dismissal Troparion. Lines 16–19 outline the festal Apodosis Liturgy: Ode 9 heirmoi and troparia from both canons on 8, troparion and kontakion of the feast, and prokeimenon LXX Ps. 44 / Lk. 1:46, 48, transitioning to folio 125r.

[^287]: Folio 125r (marked '125' in pencil at the top right; November 25, 26 & 27): Lines 1–4 conclude the Apodosis Liturgy: Epistle of the day and of the feast, Alleluia LXX Ps. 44:11, 15, Gospel, and communion verse LXX Ps. 115:4. Lines 5–13 govern November 26 (marked 'к҃ѕ' in the right margin): Our Venerable Father Alypius the Stylite ('Прп҃бнаго ѡц҃а нашего Алимпиа'): Alleluia, Troparion Tone 1 ('Тръпенїю стлъпь бысть...'), and his complete Kontakion after Ode 6, Tone 8, Podoben: "As first-fruits of nature" (Яко начатки естества): 'Ꙗко добродѣтелемь виноу · и постьникомь оудобренїе · цр҃кы славит тѧ дн҃ь · и въспѣваетъ Алимпїе · молитвами ти подаи же чьтоущимь любовїю доблести твоꙗ · и корениꙗ лютыхъ прѣгрѣшеннии избавленїа тезоимените :~' (highlighting the etymological play on Greek ἀλυπία). Lines 13–19 open November 27 (marked 'к҃з' in the right margin): The Holy Great-Martyr James the Persian ('Ст҃ого моученика Иꙗкова Перскаго'): Vespers stichera, troparion ('Моученикъ твои гн҃и въ страданіи твоемь...'), and opens his Kontakion after Ode 6, Tone 2 ('Дброкавь доброне соупроужници терпеливо дш҃ьне · и страшнаго соудища оубоꙗ сѧ перское повеленіе...'), continuing onto folio 125v.

[^288]: Folio 125v (November 27 & 28): Lines 1–4 conclude the Kontakion of St. James the Persian ('...и страхъ, ꙗко вепоплеваль еси · и ꙗвисѧ мч҃нкъ дивныи · тѣломь ꙗко лоза ѡбрѣзаемь :~') and Aposticha Glory Tone 8 ('В брою къ ѡрѫживсѧ...'). Lines 4–11 open November 28 (marked 'к҃и' in the left margin): The Holy Venerable-Martyr Stephen the New, and the Holy Martyr Irenarchus ('Ст҃го прп҃бномученника · Стефана · новаго · и ст҃ого мч҃ника · Иринарха'): Vespers stichera on 6, Glory Tone 6 ('Иже иꙁ млада къ бг҃оу...'), Aposticha Glory Tone 6 ('Еже по ѡбразоу...'), monastic troparion (as on October 17), and Matins canons (Octoechos on 4, Stephen on 6, Irenarchus on 4). Lines 11–17 provide the complete Kontakion of St. Stephen the New after Ode 6, Tone 3 ('Иꙁ неплоднаа бл҃жене корени · възрасте вѣтвь · пръвострадалца тезоимените · инокыꙗ наставникь великыи ꙗви ѡч҃е · иростине оубоꙗ вь сѧ цр҃а , нехотѧщоу хв҃ъ чтити ѡбразь · сего ради сконча сѧ мч҃ническы в вѣнець прїемь Стефане :~'). Lines 17–19 detail Aposticha Glory ('Инокоь множьство...') and Liturgy cross-reference to the common rubrics of the 28th.

[^289]: Folio 126r (marked '126' in pencil at the top right, gathering signature 'м҃в' at the bottom right; November 29 & 30): Lines 1–2 record November 29 (marked 'к҃ѳ' in the right margin): The Holy Martyrs Paramon and Venerable Acacius of the Ladder ('Стыхъ мч҃нкъ · Паромона · и прп҃бнаго · Акакыꙗ иже въ лѣствици · Аллилуиа'). Lines 2–12 open the major feast of November 30 (marked 'л҃' in the right margin): The Holy and All-Praised Apostle Andrew the First-Called ('С҃того прехвалнаго · ап҃ла Андреа пръвозванаго · празноуемь весь дн҃ь'): Vespers stichera on 6 Tone 4, Glory Tone 4 ('Рыбныи ловь...'), Aposticha Glory Tone 3 ('Сърѡдьника Петрова...'), and lines 7–12 provide his complete Dismissal Troparion, Tone 4 ('Ꙗко ап҃лоѡмь пръвозваныи · и връховнемоу истыи брать · и въселенѣноу оучителю · вл҃дцѣ въсѣхъ Андрѣа моли сѧ · миръ въселенѣи даровати · и дш҃амъ нашимъ велию мл҃ть :~'). Lines 12–18 govern Matins (canon on 6, Katavasia 'Christ is born') and give his complete Kontakion after Ode 6, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вышних ища): 'Мѫжествоу тезоименитаго бг҃огл҃ве · цр҃кы ап҃ла възхожднаго петра родника въсхвали ꙁане ꙗкоже бо древле томоу и нн҃ѣ намь ꙁоветь · прїидѣте ѡбрѣтохомь желаемаго :~'. Lines 18–19 open the Praises idiomela (3, Tone 1, on 4), running over into gathering 43 on folio 126v.

[^290]: Folio 126v (marked '126' on recto; November 30 & December 1–4): Lines 1–6 conclude the Liturgy order for the Feast of the Holy Apostle Andrew the First-Called (Nov 30): Praises Glory Tone 8 ('проповѣдника вѣрѣ...'), Great Doxology, Liturgy canon Odes 3 & 6, Prokeimenon Tone 8 (LXX Ps. 18:5, 2), Epistle to the Romans [Rom. 10:11–21], Alleluia Tone 1 (LXX Ps. 88:6), Gospel from John [Jn. 1:35–51], and communion verse LXX Ps. 18:5. Lines 7–8 introduce December in an ornate red decorative banner: 'М[Ѣ]С[Я]ЦЬ ДЕКАБРЬ ИМЯТЬ днїи · л҃а · днь имат[ь] · ч[асѡвъ] · ѳ҃ · а нощь · є҃і ·' (December has 31 days; day 9 hours, night 15 hours). Lines 9–11 record December 1 (Prophet Nahum, 'а҃ллилуиа'), December 2 (Prophet Habakkuk, 'а҃ллилуна'), and December 3 (Prophet Zephaniah, 'а҃ллилуиа'). Lines 12–18 open December 4: The Holy Great-Martyr Barbara and Our Venerable Father John of Damascus ('С҃тыѧ велико[м]ч҃ннци , варвары · и прп҃бнаго ѡ҃ца нашего їѡанна , дамаскына'): Vespers stichera on 6, Glory Tone 6 ('ѡч҃ьство авра[мле]...'), Both now Theotokion, Aposticha Glory Tone 6 ('прп҃бне ѡ҃[че]...'), Troparia ('агница твоа їс҃е...' and Glory Tone 8 'Правовѣрїю о[бразе]...'), Matins canons (Octoechos on 4, Barbara on 6, John on 4), and after Ode 3, Kontakion of St. Barbara, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Lifted Up upon the Cross" (Вознесыйся), continuing onto folio 127r.

[^291]: Folio 127r (marked '127' in pencil at the top right; December 4 — St. Barbara & St. John of Damascus): Lines 1–7 transcribe the complete Dismissal Kontakion of St. Barbara, Tone 4: 'Иже въ трⷪ҇ци бл҃гочьстно пѣваемаго · послѣдоующїи бг҃оу стратотръпнице · елинскыꙗ ѡставила еси жрътвы · посрѣдѣ же побига стражѫщи варваро · мѫчителен не оубоꙗ сѧ прѣщеньꙗ мѫжеоумнаа · велегласно въпиющи присно · тр҃цю чьтоу едино бж҃ество :~'. Lines 7–14 provide the Kontakion of St. John of Damascus after Ode 6, Tone 4: 'Пѣснописца и чьстнаго бг҃огл҃ника · цр҃кви казателѧ , и оучитела · и врагѡ[мъ] съпротивноборца · їѡанна въспоимь · ѡрѫжїе бо възѧ длꙑ крⷭ҇тъ гн҃ь · въсѧ ѡтрази ерескыꙗ льсть · и ꙗко теплъ престатель къ бг҃оу · вьсѣмь подаваеть съгрѣшенїемь прощенїе :~'. Lines 14–19 detail Praises idiomela Tone 1, Aposticha Glory Tone 6 ('Инокоь множьство...'), and Liturgy canon Odes 3 & 6, Prokeimenon Tone 4 (LXX Ps. 67:36), running over to folio 127v.

[^292]: Folio 127v (December 4 & 5): Lines 1–3 complete the Divine Liturgy for St. Barbara and St. John of Damascus: Prokeimenon verse LXX Ps. 67:27, Epistle to the Galatians [Gal. 3:23–29, pericope 208], Alleluia LXX Ps. 39:2, and Gospel from Matthew [Mt. 25:1–13]. Lines 4–19 open December 5 (marked 'є҃' in the left margin): Our Venerable and God-bearing Father Sabbas the Sanctified ('Прп҃бнаго и бг҃оноснаго ѡ҃ца нашего савы · сщ҃еннаго · празноуемь до полоудне · пѣнїе въсенощное · Аще изволить игоумень'): Great Vespers 'Blessed is the man', stichera on 8 (Tone 5 'прп҃бне ѡ҃че...'), Glory Tone 6 ('Иже по ѡбразоу...'), Both now Theotokion 1 of the tone, 3 common monastic Paremias, Aposticha Tone 5 with verses LXX Ps. 115:6 and 111:1, Glory Tone 6 ('Инокоь множьство...'), Troparion Tone 8 ('слъꙁь твоихъ...'), Matins Kathismata, Songs of Ascents Tone 4 Antiphon 1, Prokeimenon LXX Ps. 115:6, 3, Gospel from Matthew [Mt. 11:27–30], Psalm 50, and sticheron of the saint.

[^293]: Folio 128r (marked '128' in pencil at the top right; December 5 & 6): Lines 1–8 govern Matins for St. Sabbas: Canons of the Octoechos on 6 and of the saint on 8, giving his complete Kontakion after Ode 6, Tone 8: 'Ꙗко ѡ[т] младеньства б҃ви жрътва , непорочна приведе сѧ · добродѣтелью ꙗко бл҃жене · садитель былъ еси бл҃гочьстїа · тѣмьже бы[сть] прп҃бныимь оудобренїе · гражданинъ же поустыни достохвалне · тѣмь же ꙁовоу ти · радоуи же сѧ саво прѣбогате :~'. Lines 8–16 conclude St. Sabbas with Praises on 4, Glory Tone 6, Great Doxology, and Liturgy readings: Prokeimenon LXX Ps. 149:5, 1, Epistle of the 23rd Saturday [Gal. 5:22–6:2], Alleluia LXX Ps. 111:1, 2, Gospel from Luke [Lk. 6:17–23], and communion verse LXX Ps. 111:6. Lines 16–20 open December 6 (marked 'ѕ҃' in the right margin): Our Father among the Saints Nicholas, Archbishop of Myra in Lycia ('Ижевъ ст҃ы ѡ҃ца нашего , николы · архїепп҃а мѵрьликыискы[хъ] празноуемь весь днь'): Vespers 'Blessed is the man', stichera on 8, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Егда от дрѣва), continuing onto folio 128v.

[^294]: Folio 128v (December 6 — St. Nicholas of Myra): Lines 1–15 detail Great Vespers and Matins for St. Nicholas: Glory Tone 6 ('С҃тыль оудобренїе...'), Both now Theotokion 1 of the same tone, Entrance, 3 Paremias (as on Nov 13, St. John Chrysostom), Aposticha with verses LXX Ps. 115:6, 3, Glory Tone 6 ('Чл҃че бж҃їи...'), Troparion Tone 4 ('Правило вѣрѣ...'), Kathisma psalmody, Sessional hymns, Songs of Ascents Tone 4, Prokeimenon LXX Ps. 115:6, 3, Gospel from John [Jn. 10:9–16], Psalm 50, and sticheron. Lines 11–13 specify the Canons: to the Theotokos [дїнсогыллиѡдръ / Heliodoros] with heirmoi on 6, and to the saint on 8, with Katavasia 'Christ is born'. Lines 15–19 record the complete Kontakion of St. Nicholas after Ode 6, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дѣва днесь): 'Въ мѵрѣхъ ст҃ыи ст҃итель ꙗви сѧ · хв҃о бо гн҃ь , прп҃бне єѵг҃гелїе · исплънилъ еси · положи дш҃оу свою за людїе своꙗ · сп҃слъ еси неповинныꙗ ѡ[т] съмръти · сего ради с҃сти сла велїи таинникь бж҃ьꙗ бл҃годати :~', finishing on folio 129r.

[^295]: Folio 129r (marked '129' in pencil at the top right; December 6–9): Line 1 completes the Kontakion of St. Nicholas. Lines 2–9 conclude the Liturgy for St. Nicholas: Praises on 4, Glory Tone 5 ('Въстроубимь...'), Great Doxology, Liturgy canon Odes 3 & 6, Prokeimenon LXX Ps. 115:6, 3, Epistle to the Hebrews [Heb. 13:17–21], Alleluia LXX Ps. 131:9, Gospel from Luke [Lk. 6:17–23], and communion verse LXX Ps. 111:6. Lines 10–11 record December 7 (marked 'з҃'): St. Ambrose, Bishop of Milan ('амьбросьꙗ епп҃а , медиѡламьскаго · а҃ллилуиа'). Lines 12–13 record December 8 (marked 'и҃'): Our Venerable Father Patapius ('потапїа · а҃ллилуна'). Lines 13–19 open December 9 (marked 'ѳ҃'): The Conception of Saint Anne ('Зачатье ст҃ыꙗ анны · егда зачать ст҃ѫꙗ бц҃ю'): Vespers stichera on 6, Tone 4, Glory... Both now... Tone 2 ('Неизгланно...'), Aposticha of the Octoechos with Glory... Both now... Tone 2 ('Дн҃ь искорене дв҃два...'), and notes an alternate rubric combining Conception and Dedication (Sanctification of the Church) with stichera on 6, Glory Tone 6 ('Памѧ[ть] ѡбновле[нїѧ]...').

[^296]: Folio 129v (December 9 — Conception of Saint Anne & Dedication): Lines 1–4 detail the alternate Vigil order: Both now Tone 2 ('Неизгланно...'), Entrance, Prokeimenon and 3 Paremias for the Dedication, Aposticha for the Dedication on 3, Glory Tone 2 ('О̑бновленье скръ...'), Both now Tone 2 ('Ѡ корене дв҃два...'). Lines 4–11 provide the complete Dismissal Troparion of the Conception, Tone 4: 'Дн҃ь неплодьствнаꙗ съѫзы · разрѣшаютсѧ їѡакыма и а҃нноу оуслышавъ паче надежа родити еѧ ꙗвѣ · грѧдеть бо ѡтроковица · из неꙗже самь роди сѧ неѡписанныи · зелень бывь · и аг҃гломь повелѣ въпити еи · радоуисѧ ѡбрадованнаꙗ гь с тобою :~'. Lines 11–17 record Glory for the Dedication, Matins troparia, canons on 6, and after Ode 6 the complete Kontakion of the Conception, Tone 4: 'Празноуеть вселенаꙗ а҃ннино зачатье · бывшее по бз҃ѣ · ꙗко та породи , ꙗже паче слова слово рождьшѫꙗ :~'. Lines 17–19 open the Liturgy with canon Odes 3 & 6 and Prokeimenon LXX Ps. 67:36, running over to folio 130r.

[^297]: Folio 130r (marked '130' in pencil at the top right; December 9 & 10): Lines 1–4 complete the Liturgy for the Conception of St. Anne: Prokeimenon verse LXX Ps. 67:27, Epistle to the Hebrews [Heb. 9:1–7], Alleluia LXX Ps. 36:39, and Gospel from Luke [Lk. 1:39–49, 56]. Lines 4–19 open December 10 (marked 'і҃' in the right margin): The Holy Martyrs Menas, Hermogenes, and Eugraphus ('С҃тымоученикь манны · и ермогена · и евграфа'): Vespers stichera on 6, Aposticha of Octoechos with Glory to the saints, and lines 7–14 provide their complete Dismissal Troparion, Tone 8: 'Въздержаньемь стр҃ти · ѡгнепалныꙗ оумрътивше стремленьꙗ и движенїа · хв҃и мч҃нци прїꙗша бл҃гть · недоугы ѡ[т]ганати немощныимь · живи и жь по покончинїѣ · чюдодѣйствовати · въ истиноу чюдо прѣславно · ꙗко кости нагыꙗ источають исцѣленїа · слава единомоу оуснижителю бг҃оу :~'. Lines 14–19 provide the Kontakion after Ode 6, Tone 4: 'Добропѣньии гла[съ] , твоихъ словесь ерьмогене · из глѫбины погыбели н възведъ ши по ставника даени жизни · ѡноудоу же и евграфъ цр҃ꙗ лювьличникы · радꙋꙗ сѧ посѣкаеть ꙗ въ славнѫꙗ главоу · и и прилежно моли дивно · сп҃сти сѧ всѣмь любовїю поющимъ тѧ :~', concluding on folio 130v.

[^298]: Folio 130v (December 10–12): Lines 1–5 conclude the Divine Liturgy for SS. Menas, Hermogenes, and Eugraphus: Prokeimenon LXX Ps. 15:3, verse LXX Ps. 15:8, Epistle of the 27th Sunday, Alleluia LXX Ps. 33:18, and Gospel from Luke [Lk. 10:19–21]. Lines 6–13 govern December 11 (marked 'а҃і' in the left margin): Our Venerable Father Daniel the Stylite ('Прп҃бнаго ѡ҃ца нашего , данила стлъпника'): Troparion Tone 1 ('Терпѣнїю стлъпь бысть...'), Sessional hymn, and complete Kontakion after Ode 6, Tone 8, Podoben: "As First-Fruits of Nature" (Яко начатки): 'Ꙗко звѣзда многопросвѣтлаа · възьше бл҃жене на стлъпь · миръ просвѣтилъ еси · прп҃бныими дѣлы своими · и тьмоу прѣльсти ѡ[т]гналъ еси ѡ҃че · тѣмьже молимь тѧ и нн҃ѣ ѡблиетани въ ср҃дца рабъ твоихъ · незаходѧщїи свѣ[тъ] разоума :~'. Lines 14–19 open December 12 (marked 'в҃і' in the left margin): Our Venerable Father Spyridon the Wonderworker, Bishop of Trimythous ('Прп҃бнаго ѡ҃ца нашего , спиридона чютворца епп҃а триѳинска'): Vespers stichera on 6, Glory Tone 2 ('ст҃ле бж҃їь...'), Aposticha Glory Tone 1 ('Прп҃бне ѡ҃че...'), Troparion Tone 4 ('Правило вѣрѣ...'), Matins canons on 6, and opens his Kontakion after Ode 6, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the Highest" (Вышних ища), carrying over to folio 131r.

[^299]: Folio 131r (marked '131' in pencil at the top right; December 12 & 13): Lines 1–5 complete the Kontakion of St. Spyridon of Trimythous, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the Highest" (Вышних ища): 'Любовью х҃ковоу оуꙗзвивъ сѧ бл҃жене оумь впери въ ꙁарею дх҃а · дѣиственыимь видѣнїемь си · дѣꙗньа ѡбрѣте бг҃оприꙗтне · жрътвникъ бывь · просѧ всѣмь бж҃твьное сїꙗнїе :~'. Lines 5–12 conclude St. Spyridon: Aposticha Glory Tone 4, Prokeimenon LXX Ps. 115:6, 3, Epistle of December 6 [Heb. 13:17–21], Alleluia LXX Ps. 131:9, Gospel from Luke [Lk. 6:17–23], and communion verse LXX Ps. 111:6. Lines 12–19 open December 13 (marked 'г҃і' in the right margin): The Holy Martyrs Eustratius, Auxentius, Eugene, Mardarius, and Orestes, and Our Venerable Father Arsenius of Mount Latros ('С҃тыхъ моученикь , евстратиꙗ · авксентїа · еввениꙗ · мардарьꙗ · ѡреста · и прп҃бн[а]го ѡ҃ца арсенїа · иже в латрѣ'): Vespers stichera on 6, Glory Tone 6 ('Придѣте моучениколюбци...'), Aposticha Glory Tone 5 ('Мч҃нци...'), Troparia ('мч҃нци твои...' and 'слъꙁ твои...'), Matins canons on 6, and indicates after Ode 6 the Kontakion Tone 2.

[^300]: Folio 131v (December 13 & Sunday of the Holy Forefathers): Lines 1–6 complete the dismissal Kontakion of the Five Holy Martyrs Eustratius, Auxentius, Eugene, Mardarius, and Orestes, Tone 2: 'И на землꙇ добрѣ подвизавшихсѧ · и на небесѣхь славно вѣнчаныхь · є҃ · числъныꙇ застоупникь · и избавителѧ вѣрныхь · ѡ нихже евъстратїи · ꙗко сл҃нце посрѣдѣ звѣздь въсиꙗ · пѣсньми и вѣрою почтемь · молѧщихсѧ прилежно ѡ всѣхь насъ :~'. Lines 6–13 conclude the Divine Liturgy for the Martyrs: Praises stichera on Tone 4 fivefold ('пѧтерницею'), Praises Glory Tone 4 ('Бывше елинньскаго...'), Aposticha Glory Tone 4, Liturgy canon Odes 3 & 6, Prokeimenon LXX Ps. 15:3, verse LXX Ps. 15:8, Epistle of the 27th Sunday [Eph. 6:10–17], Alleluia LXX Ps. 33:18, Gospel from Luke [Lk. 12:42–48, Tuesday of 12th week], and communion verse LXX Ps. 32:1. Lines 14–19 open the rubrical order for the Sunday of the Holy Forefathers ('Неделѧ · ст҃ы праѡ҃ць · съвръша́емь · въсенощное · ѡставлꙗет же · въслѣдованье ст҃аго · егоже прилꙋчит сѧ памать · въ тоу неделѧ · поеть сѧ въ пѧтокь · на павечеральници · поють же сѧ въскрьсна · и ст҃ыхь · прѣѡ҃ць · въ сѫботу малаꙗ ве́черница'), specifying an All-Night Vigil with the transfer of any concurrent menaion commemoration to Friday Compline.

[^301]: Folio 132r (marked '132' in pencil at the top right; Sunday of the Holy Forefathers): Lines 1–13 specify Vespers for the Forefathers: Small Vespers resurrectional and Theotokos hymns; Great Vespers Kathisma 'Blessed is the man', 10 stichera at 'Lord, I have cried' (3 resurrectional, 4 Anatolian, and 3 for the Forefathers, Tone 8 'Иже въ едемѣ раи...'), Glory Tone 8 ('Прежде закона ѡц҃а...'), Both now 1st Theotokion of the current tone, Entrance stichera of the monastery's patron, Aposticha resurrectional from the Alphabet, Glory Tone 3 ('прѣѡ҃тьскыи съставь...'), Both now from the Alphabet, and Artoklasia with 'O Virgin Theotokos' twice. Lines 13–18 record the complete Dismissal Troparion of the Forefathers, Tone 2: 'Вѣрою прѣѡ҃ца ѡправдалъ еси · ꙗже ѡ[т] ꙗꙁыкъ · тѣми ѡброучивъ цр҃ковь · хвалѧт сѧ въ славѣ ст҃їи · ꙗко ѡ[т] сѣмени ихъ , есть плодъ ꙗже безъ сѣмени рождьшаа тѧ · тѣхъ мл҃твами х҃е б҃е помилоуи насъ :~'. Lines 18–19 open Matins with troparia and Epistle reading.

[^302]: Folio 132v (Sunday of the Holy Forefathers): Lines 1–8 govern Matins: Kathisma sessional hymns, 'Blessed are the undefiled' (Polyeleos / Amomos, LXX Ps. 118), Hymns of Ascents, Prokeimenon, 'Let every breath', Resurrection Gospel, 'Having beheld the resurrection of Christ', Psalm 50, resurrectional sticheron, and canons: resurrectional on 4, Theotokos on 4, and of the Three Children on 4 (Tone 8 'Водоу прошед' and 'Безначалное слово'), with Katavasia 'Christ is born'. Lines 9–15 transcribe the complete Hypakoe after Ode 3, Tone 2: 'Въ рѡсоу ѡтрокомь ѡгнь прелагаше сѧ · плачь же въ радость прелагаше сѧ · аг҃глъ бо ѡбои слоужаше чюдесѣмь · ѡвѣмь оубо въ покои претвори пещь · симь же въскрⷭ҇енїе възвѣсти тридневно · и началнице жизни нашеꙗ · Гн҃ слава тебѣ :~', followed by the Patristic reading from St. John Chrysostom on Abraham. Lines 16–19 open the complete Kontakion of the Forefathers / Three Children after Ode 6, Tone 2: 'Рѫкописаннаго ѡбраза не почътше · нꙇ неѡписанныимь сѫществомъ · въѡрѫжившесѧ треблж҃нїи въ тꙋзѣ ѡгньнѣи прославистесѧ...', carrying over to folio 133r.

[^303]: Folio 133r (marked '133' in pencil at the top right; Sunday of the Forefathers & December 14): Lines 1–4 conclude the Kontakion of the Three Children / Forefathers: '...посрѣдѣ же пламени непостоꙗннаго стоꙗще · б҃а призывасте · оускори щедры · потъщи сѧ ꙗко мл҃тивъ на помощь нашю · ꙗко можеши хотѧи :~'. Lines 4–18 govern the completion of Matins and the Divine Liturgy: Praises with 4 resurrectional and Anatolian stichera, Glory Tone 7 ('Придѣте въси вѣрно...'), Great Doxology, resurrectional troparion alone; Liturgy Prokeimenon Tone 4 (LXX Dan. 3:26, verse Dan. 3:27), Epistle of the 29th Sunday [Col. 3:4–11], Alleluia LXX Ps. 98:6, Gospel from Luke [Lk. 14:16–24, 11th Sunday], and communion verse LXX Ps. 148:1. Lines 18–19 record December 14 (marked 'д҃і' in the right margin): The Holy Martyrs Thyrsus, Leucius, Philemon, Apollonius, and Arianus ('С҃ты[хъ] мч҃нкъ , ѳирса · и левкиꙗ · филимона · и аполоніа · и арьꙗна · а҃луꙗ').

[^304]: Folio 133v (marked 'є҃і' in the left margin; December 15): Commemoration of the Holy Hieromartyr Eleutherius and Our Venerable Father Paul the Wonderworker of Mount Latros ('С҃тго сщ҃мчка · елиферьꙗ · и прп҃бнаго ѡ҃ца нашего павла чюдотворца · иже в латрѣ'). Lines 2–8 order Vespers with stichera of the saints, Aposticha of the Octoechos, common troparion ('И нравомъ причастникъ...'), and Glory Tone 4: 'Ꙗко бесплътныимь единокрѫвникъ · и въсѣмъ прп҃бныимь ѡбещникь · преславныи павьле · поемь , и молимь ти сѧ · моли въсегда ѡ насъ · ꙗко да ѡбрѧщемь мл҃ть :~'. Lines 8–15 detail Matins canons, providing the complete Kontakion of St. Eleutherius after Ode 3, Tone 2, Podoben: "The Firm..." (Твердыя): 'Оудобренїе сщ҃еныхь прп҃бьне · и прѣложенне стрⷭ҇тотръпець · въси въхвалѧюще молимь сѧ · сщ҃енномоуч҃нче елеферье · любовію памать твою празнующїи ѡ[т] бѣдъ многоразличныхь свободи · молѧ сѧ бепрестанни ѡ въсѣхь насъ :~'. Lines 15–19 begin the Kontakion of St. Paul of Latros after Ode 6, Tone 8: 'Ѿ юности бг҃омоудре ꙗже паче оума възжелѣвь · мѫжьскыи мирскыꙗ тѧжь ѡставилъ еси · и быль еси бж҃твьныимь житїемь · троици жилище...', continuing onto folio 134r.

[^305]: Folio 134r (marked '134' in pencil at the top right, with '177?' above; December 15–17): Lines 1–3 conclude the Kontakion of St. Paul of Latros: '...и просвѣтилъ еси вѣрою пристоупающихь к тобѣ · тѣм же зовемь радоуи сѧ павле ѡ[че] нашь :~'. Line 3 records December 16 (marked 'ѕ҃і' in the right margin): The Holy Prophet Haggai ('С҃тго прр҃ока , аггеꙗ · а҃ллилуиа'). Lines 4–20 open December 17 (marked 'з҃і' in the right margin): The Holy Prophet Daniel and the Three Holy Children: Ananias, Azarias, and Misael ('С҃тго прр҃ка , данила · и стыхь · г҃ · ю · ѡтрокь · ананьи · азарїи · мисаила · празноуемь весь днь'): Vespers stichera on 6, Glory Tone 2 ('Дх҃овно...'), Aposticha Glory... Both now... Tone 6 ('Даниль мѫжь желань...'). Lines 8–13 provide their complete common Troparion, Tone 2: 'Вельꙗ вѣры исправленьꙗ · въ источницѣ цѣ пламеннѣ · ꙗко на водѣ покоинѣи · ст҃ии трїе ѡтроци радовахѫ сѧ · и прр҃къ даниль львомь пастырь ꙗко ѡвцамь ꙗвлꙗше сѧ · тѣхь мл҃твами х҃е б҃е помилоуи насъ :~'. Lines 13–19 give the Matins canons and the Kontakion of St. Daniel after Ode 3, Tone 3: 'Просвѣтивьше сѧ дх҃омь · свѣтозарное твое ср҃дце · пррочьствїа бысть свѣтло прїꙗтелище · глаголющи бо ꙗко настоꙗщаа далече сѫщаа · львы оукротивый · въвръжень бывъ въ ровь · сего ради...', finishing on folio 134v.

[^306]: Folio 134v (December 17 & Saturday and Sunday before Nativity): Line 1 completes the Kontakion of St. Daniel ('...та чьтемь прр҃че бл҃жене даниле славне'). Lines 2–9 direct Matins and Liturgy for St. Daniel and the Children: Kontakion of the Children after Ode 6 ('Рѫкописаннаго...'), Liturgy Prokeimenon LXX Ps. 67:36, verse LXX Ps. 67:27, Epistle to the Hebrews [Heb. 11:33–40], Alleluia LXX Ps. 33:18, Gospel from Luke [Lk. 11:42–46, Wednesday of 10th week], and communion verse LXX Ps. 32:1. Lines 9–12 provide the concurrence rubric: if St. Daniel and the Children fall on the Sunday of the Holy Fathers, their service is sung together with the Sunday office. Lines 12–15 govern the Saturday before the Nativity: Epistle to the Galatians [Gal. 3:8–12, 27th Saturday] and Gospel from Luke [Lk. 13:18–21, pericope 21]. Lines 16–19 open the rubrics for the Sunday before the Nativity of Christ, of the Holy Fathers ('Недѣлѧ , прѣд ржⷭ҇твомь хв҃ымь , сты[хъ] ѡ҃ць'): the concurrent daily saint's office is transferred to Compline, while the Fathers' hymns are combined with the Resurrection hymns, continuing onto folio 135r.

[^307]: Folio 135r (marked '135' in pencil at the top right; Sunday of the Holy Fathers before the Nativity): Lines 1–16 detail Vespers and Matins: Small Vespers resurrectional and Theotokos hymns; Great Vespers 'Blessed is the man', 10 stichera at 'Lord, I have cried' (3 resurrectional, 1 Anatolian, 3 Forefeast, 3 Fathers Tone 8 'Иже въ едемѣ...'), Glory Tone 6 ('Даниль мѫжь желань...'), Both now Forefeast ('Вертпе оуготови...'), 3 Paremias (as on 7th Sunday after Pascha), Litiya Dogmatica, Glory Tone 1 ('Слоужащемь звѣздамь...'), Both now Tone 1 ('Слыши нбо...'), Aposticha with Glory Tone 2 ('Радоуйте сѧ прр҃ци чт҃нїи...'), Both now Forefeast ('Севрѣма наста...'), Artoklasia troparia ('O Virgin Theotokos' twice, 'Great are the achievements' once), Epistle reading, and Matins troparia. Lines 16–19 govern Matins psalmody: Kathismata, 'Blessed are the undefiled' (Polyeleos / Amomos, LXX Ps. 118) with troparia, Tone 8 psalmody, Ascents, Prokeimenon, 'Let every breath', and Resurrection Gospel.

[^308]: Folio 135v (Sunday of the Holy Fathers before the Nativity): Lines 1–5 order the Canons: Tone 6 'Волною морьскою' on 8, and Forefeast on 4 (Ode 1 'Christ is born'), Katavasia 'Christ is born'; the rubric notes that the resurrectional canon of the Octoechos is omitted because the tone canon already contains two resurrectional troparia. Lines 6–10 transcribe the complete Hypakoe after Ode 3, Tone 8: 'Аг҃глъ ѡтрокѡмъ ѡброси пещь · нн҃ѣ же женамь плачь възбранаше глагола · что принесосте мѵро · кого ищете въ гробѣ · въскр҃се хс҃ б҃ъ · еко животы исп҃с родоу чл҃чьскомоу :~'. Lines 10–19 record Kontakion 'Рѫкописаннаго...', Exapostilaria, Praises (4 resurrectional, 3 Fathers Tone 8 'Ижевъ едемѣ...', with verse LXX Dan. 3:26 and idiomelon Tone 8, Glory Tone 8 'Законныи оученьи...', Both now 'Most blessed art thou'), Great Doxology, resurrectional troparion alone, and opens the Divine Liturgy order, running over to folio 136r.

[^309]: Folio 136r (marked '136' in pencil at the top right; Sunday of the Holy Fathers, Dec 18–20 & Repose of St. Peter of Moscow): Lines 1–7 complete the Divine Liturgy for the Sunday of the Holy Fathers: Octoechos and Fathers canon Ode 3 on 4, Prokeimenon Tone 4 (LXX Dan. 3:26, verse Dan. 3:27), Epistle to the Hebrews [Heb. 11:9–10, 17–23, 32–40], Alleluia LXX Ps. 43:2, verse Ps. 43:8, Gospel from Matthew [Mt. 1:1–25, Genealogy of Christ], and communion verses LXX Ps. 148:1 and 32:1. Lines 8–10 give the rubrical reference: if the Sunday of the Fathers falls on December 24 (Christmas Eve), refer to the Rubrical Chapters (*Skazaniya*). Lines 10–11 record December 18 (marked 'и҃і' in the right margin): The Holy Martyr Sebastian and companions ('а҃ллуꙗ'). Lines 11–12 record December 19 (marked 'ѳ҃і' in the right margin): The Holy Martyr Boniface ('а҃ллуна'). Lines 12–14 open December 20 (marked 'к҃' in the right margin): The Holy Hieromartyr Ignatius the God-Bearer, beginning the Forefeast of the Nativity. Lines 14–19 provide a rare and momentous historical chronicle note in red cinnabar: 'Въ тъи днь · престави сѧ · пресщ҃еныи , митрополитъ , петръ · всеꙗ роуси · въ г҃ · ча[съ] · нощи · в лѣто , ҂ѕ҃ · тысеща · ѡ҃ · л҃д · пасъ цр҃ковь бж҃їю лѣтъ · и҃і · и мц҃ь · ѕ҃ · и положень бы[сть] · въ градѣ москвѣ · въ цр҃кви оуспеньꙗ стыꙗ бца ·' (On that day reposed the Most Consecrated Metropolitan Peter of All Rus', at the 3rd hour of the night, in the year 6834 [1326 AD]; he pastured the Church of God for 18 years and 6 months, and was laid to rest in the city of Moscow, in the Church of the Dormition of the Holy Theotokos).

[^310]: Folio 136v (December 20: Repose of St. Peter of Moscow & St. Ignatius the God-Bearer, Forefeast of Nativity): Lines 1–3 complete the historic chronicle entry regarding the repose and burial of Metropolitan Peter of Moscow: 'юже самъ съзьдалъ · и подають исцѣлени[е] · ѿ чьстны[хъ] его мощи · приходѧщи[мъ], съ вѣрою · и до сего дни' (which [church of the Dormition] he himself had built; and healings are granted from his honorable relics unto those who approach with faith even unto this day). Lines 3–11 govern Vespers for December 20 (marked 'к҃' on 136r): 3 stichera of the Forefeast, Tone 4 ('прѣпразноуемь...'), 3 of St. Ignatius ('Бг҃оносче...'), Aposticha idiomela of the Forefeast on Tone 2 with Habakkuk verses (LXX Hab. 3:3 and 3:2), Glory Tone 4, Both now Tone 2 ('Севрѣма наста...'). Lines 11–18 record the complete Dismissal Troparion of the Forefeast of the Nativity, Tone 4: 'Готовисѧ виѳлеѡме ѿвръзисѧ всѣмь едемь · красоуисѧ єфраньѳа · ꙗко древо животное въ врьтепѣ процвѣте ѿ дв҃ы · раи бо тоꙗ чрево ꙗвисѧ мысльнын · въ немьже бж҃твьныи садъ · ѿ негоже ꙗдьше живи боудемь · и не ꙗко адамъ оумремь · Хс҃ ражаетсѧ , прежде падышии въскрѣсенти ѡбразъ :~'. Line 18–19 prescribe Glory to St. Ignatius ('И нравомь...'), Both now of the Forefeast, litany, and dismissal.

[^311]: Folio 137r (marked '137' in pencil at the top right; December 20: St. Ignatius & Forefeast): Lines 1–4 detail Matins: Kathisma sessional hymns, canon of the Forefeast on 8 (heirmoi twice), and canon of St. Ignatius on 6, with the significant rubric: 'И ѿ нн҃ѣ же не гл҃и · донелѣже и празникъ · хв҃а ржⷭ҇тва ѿдастьсѧ' (And from now on do not chant [the Octoechos] until the feast of Christ's Nativity is leave-taken). Lines 5–10 record the complete Kontakion of St. Ignatius, Tone 3: 'Свѣтлыхь подвигъ твоихъ · свѣтоносныи днь · проповѣдаетъ всѣмь · въ врьтепѣ рожьшагосѧ · того жажѧ любовію напитатисѧ потщилсѧ еси ѿ звѣріи снѣдень быти · сего ради бг҃оносець наречень бысть игнатье :~'. Lines 10–16 record the complete Kontakion of the Forefeast by St. Romanos the Melodist, Tone 3: 'Дв҃а днь превѣчное слово · въ врьтепь грѧдетъ родити неизреченно · ликоуи вселенаꙗ слышавьши · и прослави съ аг҃глы · и пастырьми · хотѧща ꙗвити сѧ ѿроча младо превѣчныи б҃ъ :~'. Lines 16–19 conclude Matins with the Praises (Forefeast Tone 6 'аг҃гльскы'), Aposticha (Tone 4 idiomela, Glory Tone 1, Both now Tone 6).

[^312]: Folio 137v (General Typikon Rule on the Octoechos & December 21: Holy Martyr Juliana of Nicomedia): Lines 1–6 articulate the foundational seasonal liturgical rule: 'Подобаеть же вѣдати · сице бываеть, въслѣдованїе и до канѡна · рожества хв҃а · ѡхтаикъ не поет сѧ · донелѣже и кр҃щнье проводит сѧ · се же е[сть] · ѿ к҃, го дни декабрѧ, мц҃а · и до , д҃і · генварѧ · въ нже ѿдаетсѧ празникъ · бг҃оꙗвленьꙗ' (It is fitting to know: thus is the order even until the canon of the Nativity of Christ; the Octoechos is not sung until the Baptism [Theophany] is conducted, that is, from the 20th day of the month of December unto the 14th day of January, on which the Feast of Theophany is leave-taken). Lines 6–11 prescribe the Divine Liturgy for Dec 20: Prokeimenon LXX Ps. 63:11, verse Ps. 63:2, Epistle [Rom. 8:28–39], Alleluia LXX Ps. 91:13, Gospel pericope [Lk. 4:31–36, Capernaum healing], and communion verse. Lines 12–19 open December 21 (marked 'к҃а' in the left margin): Holy Martyr Juliana of Nicomedia ('С҃тыꙗ мч҃енницю оульꙗны'): Vespers, Matins Kathismata, canons (Forefeast on 8, St. Juliana on 4), and the opening lines of her Kontakion after Ode 3, Tone 3: 'Дѣвьствоу доброта присно · и лоученникомь вѣнець · ꙇоульꙗніи нн҃ѣ вѣнчасѧ · дароующиимь въ недꙋзѣхь болѣзнемь...', continuing onto folio 138r.

[^313]: Folio 138r (marked '138' in pencil at the top right; December 21 & December 22: Holy Great Martyr Anastasia): Lines 1–4 conclude the Kontakion of St. Juliana ('...исцѣленіе · и сп҃сеніе прибѣгающимь къ кроувоу твоемоу съ оусрьдіемь · бж҃твьныꙗ славы полоучимь жизнь вѣчнѫꙗ :~'), followed by Matins completion. Lines 5–19 record December 22 (marked 'к҃в' in the right margin): Holy Great Martyr Anastasia the Deliverer from Potions ('С҃тыꙗ велико[мч҃нцы] , анастасїи'): Vespers stichera on 6, common troparion 'Агница твоꙗ іс҃е', Matins troparia, Kathismata, canons (Forefeast on 6, St. Anastasia on 6). Lines 12–17 transcribe her complete Kontakion after Ode 3, Tone 2: 'Иже въ искоушенїнхъ , и скръбехь сѫщеи · къ твоеи цр҃кви притѣчюще · прїемлѧть чⷭ҇тнаа исцѣленїа · ѿ тебе живоущиꙗ бж҃твьныꙗ бл҃гдти · анастасїе · ты бо даровни прⷭ҇но источаеши исцѣленїа :~'. Lines 17–19 conclude Matins and open the Divine Liturgy order.

[^314]: Folio 138v (December 22–24: Ten Martyrs of Crete & Eve of Nativity / St. Eugenia): Lines 1–4 conclude the Divine Liturgy for St. Anastasia: Liturgy canon Odes 3 & 6, Prokeimenon LXX Ps. 67:36, verse LXX Ps. 67:27, Epistle [Gal. 3:23–29], Alleluia LXX Ps. 39:2, Gospel from Luke [Lk. 10:19–21], and communion verse. Lines 4–16 govern December 23 (marked 'к҃г' in the left margin): The Ten Holy Martyrs of Crete ('С҃ты[хъ] мч҃нкъ , і҃ · иже въ критѣ'): Vespers, Matins canons (Forefeast on 8, Martyrs on 4), and their complete Kontakion after Ode 3, Tone 4: 'Звѣзда въсиꙗ · мч҃нчьское чьстное страданіе · преѡ̈зарѧющи намь · иже въ вертпѣ крыемаго · егоже дв҃а ꙗже безъ сѣменно породи :~', followed by Praises and Aposticha. Lines 16–19 open December 24 (marked 'к҃д' in the left margin): The Holy Nun-Martyr Eugenia ('С҃тыꙗ прп҃бныꙗ мч҃нци , евгеныи') and the Eve of the Nativity, providing the opening lines of the Troparion of the Forefeast, Tone 4: 'Съписасѧ иногда съ старцемъ іѡсифомъ · ꙗко ѿ сѣмене дв҃д · въ виѳлеѡмѣ...', running over to folio 139r.

[^315]: Folio 139r (marked '139' in pencil at the top right; December 24: Eve of Nativity / St. Eugenia & Typikon Rules for the Nativity Season): Lines 1–5 conclude the Troparion of the Forefeast: 'Марїꙗ · дьвъчревоносащи безсѣменно рожество · наста же времѧ ржⷭ҇тва и мѣсто не бѣ ѡбители · но ꙗко красна палата вертепъ цр҃ци показа · Хс҃ ражаетсѧ прежде падшии въскрⷭ҇сити ѡбразъ :~'. Lines 5–11 govern Matins and transcribe the complete Kontakion of the Nun-Martyr Eugenia after Ode 3, Tone 2: 'Бл҃городїа правитѧ красолѣпно · ꙋкрашенꙋ ѡбници въ гѫрабьинъ зракъ ѡ[т] дв҃ы · соединили кодвы сщ҃еносѣ · показа сїꙗющꙋ дв҃амь вѣченоснꙋю · ꙗво истинно бл҃городїа бж҃твную славꙋ возлюбивши :~'. Lines 11–14 conclude Matins with Praises, Aposticha, Troparion, litany, and dismissal. Lines 14–19 record the seasonal psalmody and fasting rubrics governing the period from Christmas Eve through the Apodosis of Theophany (January 14): 'Вѣдомо же боуди ѿ сего дни ендо · д҃і · дни · генваря · мц҃а вон же ѿдаетсѧ празникъ · бг҃оꙗвленїꙗ · стїслови на оу́трини по в҃ каѳїзмѣ · а на вечерни · г҃ · а · р҃ · аввага · ѿправꙗет же нде сѧстаеть · к҃г · до · ст҃ьнеци · поемь на вечерни · павечерница же · малаꙗ · ѡпоущилницѧ бѣ · безъ ꙗзыка :~', mandating two Kathismata at Matins, ordinary psalmody at Vespers, and Small Compline without bows/metanoias.

[^316]: Folio 139v (December 24: The Royal Hours of the Nativity of Christ — First Hour): Lines 1–4 describe the opening ceremonial gathering and vesting: 'Въ вторыи , ча[съ] · дни · оу́дарѧють въ било и събираемьсѧ въ цр҃ковь · їереи же · и дьꙗконь · ѡблекꙋтсѧ в ризы · Посемь · бл҃внькьшоу · їерею · начинаемь · а҃ · го · ча[са]' (At the second hour of the day they strike the semantron [bilo] and we gather into church; and the priest and deacon vest in vestments; after this, the priest having given the blessing, we begin the First Hour). Lines 4–9 detail the initial prayers and the three appointed Psalms of the First Hour of Nativity: Psalm 5 ('Гл҃ы моꙗ внуши'), Psalm 44 ('Ѿригну ср҃дце мое'), and Psalm 45 ('Б҃ъ намь прибѣжище'), with Glory... Both now... Alleluia. Lines 10–19 specify the Great Litany and the antiphonal chanting of the troparia between the two choirs: Troparion Tone 8 ('Виѳлеѡме оуготовисѧ...'), Habakkuk verses (LXX Hab. 3:2 and 3:3) with Troparion Tone 3, carrying over to folio 140r.

[^317]: Folio 140r (marked '140' in pencil at the top right; The Royal Hours of the Nativity — First Hour continued): Lines 1–4 conclude the antiphonal hymnody of the First Hour with Glory... Both now..., Tone 8 ('Сїꙗ гл҃ть їѡсифь...'), followed by the Little Litany. Lines 4–13 prescribe the readings of the First Hour: Prokeimenon Tone 4 (LXX Ps. 2:7, verse Ps. 2:8); Prophecy of Micah [Micah 5:2–4: 'Тако гл҃еть гь ты виѳлеѡмь землє їоудова...']; Epistle to the Hebrews [Heb. 1:1–12: 'Мнѡгочастнѣ...']; and the Gospel from Matthew [Mt. 1:18–25: 'Іѵ Хв҃о рожество...']. Lines 14–19 detail the concluding order: litany, Troparion of the Forefeast ('Списасѧ...'), verse LXX Ps. 118:133 ('Стопы моꙗ направи...'), Trisagion, 'Our Father', Kontakion 'Today the Virgin', 12 Lord have mercies, priestly blessing ('Б҃е оущедри ны...'), and a brief period of silent prayer.

[^318]: Folio 140v (The Royal Hours of the Nativity of Christ — Third Hour): Lines 1–4 complete the silent prayer and opening of the Third Hour with the priestly exclamation 'Бл҃вень б҃ъ нашь', Trisagion, and 3 metanoias ('Прїдѣте поклонимсѧ'). Lines 4–7 appoint the three Psalms of the Third Hour of Nativity: Psalm 66 ('Б҃е оу́щедри ны'), Psalm 86 ('Ѡснованье его на горахъ'), and Psalm 50 ('Помилоуи мѧ б҃е'), with Alleluia. Lines 7–13 order the Great Litany, antiphonal Troparion on Tone 6 ('Се б҃ъ нашь...'), verse, and Little Litany. Lines 14–19 prescribe the readings: Prokeimenon Tone 4 (LXX Is. 9:6); Prophecy of Baruch / Jeremiah [Baruch 3:36–4:4: 'Се б҃ъ нашь и не приложитсѧ инъ къ немоу...']; Epistle to the Galatians [Gal. 3:23–29]; and Gospel from Luke [Lk. 2:1–20: 'Въ ты дни изыде повеленье ѿ кесаря августа...'], concluding with the litany.

[^319]: Folio 141r (marked '141' in pencil at the top right; The Royal Hours of the Nativity — Third Hour conclusion & Sixth Hour): Lines 1–4 conclude the Third Hour: Troparion 'Списасѧ', verse LXX Ps. 67:20 ('Гь б҃ъ бл҃венъ'), Trisagion, 'Our Father', Kontakion 'Today the Virgin', 12 Lord have mercies, and dismissal. Lines 5–10 govern the bell-ringing and assembly for the Sixth and Ninth Hours: 'Въ начило · ѕ҃ · го · часа · знаменаеть · ѕ҃ · муно · ѿ му · и събираемсѧ къ цр҃кови · попь · и дьꙗконь ѡблекꙋтсѧ · и посемь · два · часа · ѕ҃ · и · ѳ҃ · бл҃внквшу їерею...' (At the beginning of the 6th Hour he tolls 6 strokes, and we gather into church; the priest and deacon vest; and after this the two Hours, the 6th and the 9th, the priest having given the blessing: 'O Heavenly King', Trisagion, 'Our Father', 12 Lord have mercies, 3 metanoias). Lines 11–14 designate the three Psalms of the Sixth Hour of Nativity: Psalm 71 ('Б҃е судъ твои'), Psalm 131 ('Помѧни гн҃ дв҃да'), and Psalm 90 ('Живыи въ помощь'), with Alleluia and Great Litany. Lines 15–19 detail the antiphonal hymnody: Troparion Tone 1 ('Прїидѣте вѣрнїи...'), Psalm verse LXX Ps. 71:10, Troparion Tone 4 ('Слыши нбо...'), and Psalm verse LXX Ps. 21:28.

[^320]: Leaf p271 marks the conclusion of the Sixth Hour and the opening of the Ninth Hour of the Royal Hours for the Eve of the Nativity of Christ. The Sixth Hour features the Troparion of Tone 5 (*Come, ye Christ-bearing people* / *Прїидите христоноснїи людїе*), Prokeimenon from LXX Ps. 109:3 (*From the womb before the morning star*), Prophecy from Isaiah 7:10–16; 8:1–4, 9–10, the Epistle from Hebrews 1:10–2:3 (appointed for the 2nd Sunday of the Great Fast), and the Gospel from Matthew 2:1–12 (*When Jesus was born in Bethlehem of Judea*). The hour concludes with the Troparion of the Forefeast (*She was enrolled once* / *Списася иногда*), the Sessional hymn (*Quickly go before* / *Скоро предвари*), the Kontakion (*Today the Virgin* / *Дѣва днесь*), and a period of silent secret prayer (*молящеся въ себе*) before opening the Ninth Hour.

[^321]: Leaf p272 contains the Royal Hours Ninth Hour. In this early Ruthenian recension, the three appointed psalms are Psalm 109 (*The Lord said unto my Lord*), Psalm 84 (*Lord, Thou hast been favourable unto Thy land*), and Psalm 85 (*Bow down Thine ear, O Lord*). The service appoints the Troparia of Tone 1 (*When Joseph went up to Bethlehem*), Tone 6 (*Today He is born of the Virgin*), and Tone 8 (*O lawgivers of Israel* / *Законоположници*), followed by the Prokeimenon from LXX Ps. 86:5, 1 (*A man shall say: Mother Zion*), the 7th Paremia from Isaiah 9:6–7 (*A child is born unto us*), the Epistle to the Hebrews cross-referenced to the Synaxis of the Theotokos on December 26 (Heb. 2:11–18), and the Gospel from Matthew 2:13–23 (*When the wise men were departed*).

[^322]: Leaf p273 concludes the Ninth Hour with Dan. 3:34 (*Deliver us not up utterly for Thy holy Name's sake*) and sets forth the Typika (*Изобразительны*) with Psalms 102 and 145, the Beatitudes, and the Kontakion of the Forefeast. It then provides the rubrics for Great Vespers joined to the Divine Liturgy of St. Basil the Great on the Eve of the Nativity, rung at the 9th or 10th hour. Uniquely, the priest completes the Proskomedia during the chanting of the stichologia of *Lord, I have cried* (Tone 2) while the deacon censes the church.

[^323]: Leaf p274 details the Vespers and Liturgy of St. Basil for Christmas Eve: 8 stichera on *Lord, I have cried* (including *Come, let us rejoice in the Lord*, *When the Lord Jesus was born*, *Thy kingdom*, *What shall we offer Thee*, and the Doxastikon *When Augustus reigned upon earth* by the Nun Cassia), Little Entrance with the Gospel, Old Testament Paremias interspersed with sung festal troparia, Trisagion (*Holy God*), Epistle to the Hebrews 1:1–12 (appointed for the 1st Saturday of the Great Fast), Alleluia with verses from LXX Ps. 109:1–3, Gospel from Luke 2:1–20, and the Liturgy of St. Basil with Communion verse from LXX Ps. 148:1 (*Praise the Lord from the heavens*). The rubric introduces the rubrical transfer when Christmas Eve falls on a Saturday or Sunday.

[^324]: Leaf p275 details the liturgical dispensation when the Eve of the Nativity of Christ falls on a Saturday or Sunday: the fast is mitigated, the Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom is celebrated in the morning, and the brethren consume bread and wine in church after the dismissal, while the Liturgy of St. Basil the Great is deferred to the Feast Day itself (December 25). Vespers is sung in the late afternoon (at the 10th hour) with Kathisma 1 (*Blessed is the man*), the Old Testament paremias, the Little Litany without *Holy God*, the Apostle from Galatians 3:23–29 (*Before faith came*), and the Gospel from Luke 2:1–20.

[^325]: Leaf p276 concludes the Eve meal dispensation (permitting oil and two cups of wine in the refectory) and outlines the precedence rules when the Nativity of Christ falls on Sunday: all Octoechos Resurrection elements (Sunday canons, Resurrection Gospel, *Having seen the Resurrection of Christ*, and *Holy is the Lord our God*) are completely superseded by the Feast, with only 3 festal kathismata sung. In the first hour of the night, the great bell is sounded for the All-Night Vigil beginning with Great Compline (*Павечерница великая*), followed by the Litiya procession singing the festal idiomela of Tone 1, Tone 5 (*The Persian magi*), and Tone 6 (*The angels dance*).

[^326]: Leaf p277 contains the Aposticha of Great Compline / Litiya, the Blessing of the Loaves (Artoklasia) with the Festal Troparion sung 3 times (Tone 4, *Thy Nativity, O Christ our God* / *Рожество Твое, Христе Боже наш*), and a patristic reading from St. John Chrysostom's Homilies on Matthew. It then sets forth the order of Festal Matins: *God is the Lord*, Kathismata 20 and 21, Polyeleos, Festal Sessional hymns, Gradual Hymns of Tone 4, Gospel from Matthew 1:18–25, Psalm 50, and the two festive canons of Tone 1 by St. Cosmas of Maiuma (*Christ is born, give ye glory*) and St. John of Damascus (*Of old, the Master that worked wonders saved a people*).

[^327]: Leaf p278 details the Matins canons and post-Ode hymnography: katavasia heirmoi sung by both choirs together, the Hypakoe of Tone 8 (*The firstfruits of the Gentiles* / *Начатокъ языкъ*), St. Gregory the Theologian's Oration 38 on the Theophany or Nativity (*Christ is born, glorify Him*), the Kontakion of Tone 3 by St. Romanos the Melodist (*The Virgin today gives birth to the Transcendent in essence* / *Дѣва днесь Пресущественнаго раждаетъ*), St. Basil the Great's Homily on the Nativity, distribution of lit candles unto the brethren at Ode 9 with omission of the Magnificat (*More honorable than the Cherubim*), the Exapostilarion (*He hath visited us from on high* / *Посѣтилъ ны есть съ высоты*), and 4 idiomela at the Praises.

[^328]: Leaf p279 marks the conclusion of Matins with the Praises (Tone 4, *Rejoice, ye righteous* / *Веселитеся праведнїи*), Glory in Tone 6 (*When the time of Thy coming upon earth was fulfilled*), Both now in Tone 2 (*Today Christ is born of the Virgin in Bethlehem*), the Great Doxology, anointing of the brethren with holy oil by the hegumen, and the opening of the Christmas Day Divine Liturgy (December 25). Celebrated early on account of the vigil, the Liturgy features the Festal Antiphons: Antiphon 1 (LXX Ps. 110 with refrain *By the prayers of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us*) and Antiphon 2 (LXX Ps. 111 with refrain *Save us, O Son of God, who wast born of the Virgin, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia*).

[^329]: Leaf p280 concludes the Festal Antiphons of the Christmas Day Liturgy: Antiphon 2 verses from LXX Ps. 111:1–4 with the hymn *Only-begotten Son*, and Antiphon 3 from LXX Ps. 109:1–3 with the Festal Troparion (*Thy Nativity, O Christ our God*). It concludes with the Little Entrance verse from LXX Ps. 109:3–4 (*From the womb before the morning star have I begotten Thee. The Lord hath sworn and will not repent: Thou art a Priest unto ages after the order of Melchizedek*), followed by the Festal Troparion.

[^330]: Leaf p281 details the conclusion of the Christmas Day Divine Liturgy (December 25) and Great Vespers of the feast celebrated in the late afternoon. The Liturgy concludes with the festal Kontakion of Romanos the Melodist (*The Virgin today* / *Дѣва днесь*), the baptismal hymn *As many as have been baptized into Christ* (Gal. 3:27) replacing the Trisagion, Prokeimenon from LXX Ps. 65:4, 1, the Epistle from Galatians 4:4–7 (*When the fulness of time was come*), Alleluia from LXX Ps. 88:6 and Ps. 18:3, the Gospel from Matthew 2:1–12 (*When Jesus was born in Bethlehem of Judea*), and the Communion Hymn from LXX Ps. 110:9 (*The Lord hath sent redemption unto His people*). Following the monastic refectory consolation, Great Vespers is sung without the opening stichologia, featuring 6 festal stichera on *Lord, I have cried* (Tone 2, *Come, let us rejoice in the Lord*), the festal Doxastikon (*Glory to God in the highest*), the Entrance, and the Great Prokeimenon of Tone 7 from LXX Ps. 76:14–15, 11–12 (*Who is a great God as our God?*).

[^331]: Leaf p282 continues the evening Aposticha of Christmas Day with idiomela in Tone 8 (*A glorious wonder* / *Преславное чюдо*) and verses from LXX Ps. 109:1, 3, concluding with Tone 1 (*When the Magi had come together in Bethlehem*). It then introduces December 26: the Synaxis of the Most Holy Theotokos (*Съборъ святыя Богородица*), the commemoration of St. Joseph the Betrothed, and Hieromartyr Euthymius, Bishop of Sardis (d. 824). The Matins structure prescribes the festal Troparion (Tone 4, 3 times), two Kathismata, Sessional hymns of the feast, and the two festive canons of St. Cosmas and St. John (first to 6 with heirmoi doubled, second with heirmos to 6; Katavasia *Christ is born*). Uniquely, the Horologion notes the total suppression of the Biblical Odes and Magnificat through the entire post-festive period up to the Apodosis of Theophany. Following Ode 6, the codex appoints the Idiomelon Kontakion in Tone 6 (*He who was born before the morning star*).

[^332]: Leaf p283 records the full text of the Kontakion for the Synaxis of the Theotokos (Tone 6: *He who was born of the Father without mother before the morning star* / *Преже дньница от Отца без матере родися*). It details Matins Praises (Tone 4, 4 stichera, Glory in Tone 6 *Today the Invisible*, Both now in Tone 2 *Today Christ*), Great Doxology, and the Divine Liturgy for December 26: Beatitudes from festal Odes 3 and 6, Kontakion, Prokeimenon from the Magnificat (Lk. 1:46, 48), the Epistle to the Hebrews 2:11–18 (*Both He that sanctifies and they who are sanctified are all of one*), Alleluia from LXX Ps. 131:8 (*Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest*), and Gospel from Matthew 2:13–23 (the Flight into Egypt). It then outlines the Saturday after the Nativity: Liturgy Prokeimenon from LXX Ps. 44:18, 11 (*I will remember Thy name*), Epistle from 1 Tim. 6:11–16, Alleluia from LXX Ps. 131:1 (*Remember, O Lord, David*).

[^333]: Leaf p284 concludes the Liturgy of the Saturday after Nativity with the Gospel from Matthew 9:27–35 (Healing of the two blind men) and Communion verse from LXX Ps. 115:4 (*The cup of salvation*). It then introduces the rubrics for the Sunday after the Nativity of Christ (*Недѣля по рожествѣ Христовѣ*), commemorating the Holy Ancestors: Righteous Joseph the Betrothed, David the King, and James the Brother of the Lord (*брата Господня*). Great Vespers features Kathisma 1 (*Blessed is the man*), 3 Resurrection stichera, 4 of the feast (Tone 2, *Come, let us rejoice*), 3 of the holy Ancestors (Tone 1 Podoben: *Thou who bearest flesh*), Doxastikon in Tone 6 (*Rejoicing, we celebrate*), Entrance, Resurrection sticheron with the Alphabetical Stichera (*азбуковницы*), Doxastikon in Tone 6 (*Jerusalem, rejoice*), Artoklasia, and the Troparion of the Holy Ancestors in Tone 2 (*Proclaim the marvelous wonders unto David* / *Благовѣствуй, Іосифе, Давиду чудеса*).

[^334]: Leaf p285 completes the Troparion of the Holy Ancestors (*Proclaim the marvelous wonders*) and details Sunday Matins: Resurrection Troparion twice, Glory of the saints, Both now of the feast; Kathisma sessional hymns; Psalm 118 with troparia; Hypakoe; Polyeleos; festal sessional hymn; Graduals; Resurrection Gospel; Psalm 50; and Canons (Resurrection to 4, Theotokos to 2, Righteous Ancestors to 4). After Ode 3, it sets forth the full text of the Kontakion of the Holy Ancestors in Tone 1: *The prophetic choir celebrates divinely the marvel wrought unto thee, O Virgin* (*Ликъ пророчьскый празднуетъ божественнѣ*), praising the Incarnation, angels, shepherds, magi, and Joseph crying aloud the wonders of David. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the feast; after Ode 9, two Exapostilaria; Praises with 4 Resurrection stichera and festal stichera in Tone 4.

[^335]: Leaf p286 concludes Sunday Matins with the Doxastikon from Joel 2:30 / Acts 2:19 (*Blood and fire*), the Great Doxology, and the Divine Liturgy of the Sunday after Nativity: Beatitudes to 6 and saints' Ode 6 to 4, Prokeimenon from LXX Ps. 67:36, 27 (*God is marvelous in His saints*), Epistle from Galatians 1:11–19 (appointed for the 20th Sunday after Pentecost), Alleluia from LXX Ps. 131:1, Gospel from Matthew 2:13–23, and Communion verse from LXX Ps. 148:1. The leaf then records crucial Typikon precedence rules: if the Nativity of Christ falls on Sunday, the commemoration of the Holy Ancestors (Joseph, David, and James) cannot be observed on the following Sunday (January 1) because of the double celebration of the Circumcision of Christ and St. Basil the Great, nor on Saturday (December 31) because of the Apodosis of the Nativity; therefore, their commemoration is transferred to December 26. Furthermore, it regulates the distribution of Gospels when two Sundays occur between Christmas and Theophany (Matthew 2:13–23 on the first; Mark 1:1–8 on the Sunday before Theophany).

[^336]: Leaf p287 details the service for December 27: the Feast of the Holy Protomartyr and Archdeacon Stephen (*Святаго первомученика и архидіакона Стефана*) and of Venerable Theophanes the Branded (*Ѳеофана Начертаннаго* / Graptos, d. ca. 845). At Vespers: stichera for St. Theophanes, Glory in Tone 2 (*Unto the King and Master*), Aposticha of the feast, Troparion of the Protomartyr, Glory for St. Theophanes (*Guide of Orthodoxy*, Tone 8, Oct. 11). Matins features festal Kathismata, canons of the feast to 6, Protomartyr to 4, and St. Theophanes to 4. After Ode 3, the Horologion transcribes the full text of St. Stephen's famous Kontakion by St. Romanos the Melodist in Tone 3: *Yesterday the Master was revealed unto us in the flesh, and today the servant departs from the flesh* (*Владыка вчера намъ плотью явися, и рабъ днесь изъ плоти исходитъ*).

[^337]: Leaf p288 concludes the service of St. Stephen with the Praises (Tone 2 and 4, Glory in Tone 5 *The first-martyr and apostle*), Aposticha, and Divine Liturgy: Prokeimenon from LXX Ps. 18:5, 2 (*Into all the earth*), Epistle from Acts 6:8–7:5, 47–60, Alleluia from LXX Ps. 88:6, Gospel from Matthew 21:33–42 (Parable of the Wicked Vinedressers), and Communion verse from LXX Ps. 18:5. It then provides the rubrics for December 28: the Holy 20,000 Martyrs burned in Nicomedia (under Maximian in 302; Troparion *Passion-bearers of the Lord* and Kontakion of Tone 2); and introduces December 29: the Holy 14,000 Innocents slain by Herod in Bethlehem (*Святыхъ младенець иже от Ирода избьены за Христа*), and Venerable Marcellus, Abbot of the Akoimetoi ("Unsleeping Ones") Monastery in Constantinople (d. 485).

[^338]: Leaf p289 continues December 29 with the Troparia: of the Holy Innocents (*Passion-bearers of the Lord*, cross-referenced to Oct. 24) and St. Marcellus (*In thee, O father*, Oct. 1). Matins appoints canons of the feast (to 6), Innocents (to 4), and St. Marcellus (to 4). After Ode 3, it records the complete text of two Kontakia: first, to St. Marcellus in Tone 8 (*As a radiant star shining from Edessa unto us, O blessed one...* / *Яко звѣзда красносная от Едеса просвѣтналъ еси намъ, блажене*); second, to the Holy Innocents in Tone 6 (*When the King was born in Bethlehem, the magi from Persia arrived with gifts... but Herod is troubled and mows down the infants like wheat...* / *Въ Виѳлеомѣ рождьшюся Царю, волсви от Персъ съ дары приходятъ*).

[^339]: Leaf p290 concludes December 29 with Matins Praises, Aposticha, and Divine Liturgy for the Holy Innocents: Beatitudes from festal Ode 3 and Innocents Ode 6, Prokeimenon from LXX Ps. 112:1 (*Praise, O ye servants, the Lord*), Epistle from Hebrews 2:11–18 (repeated from Dec. 26), Alleluia from LXX Ps. 78:3 (*Their blood have they shed like water*), Gospel in order: Matthew 2:13–23, and Communion verse from LXX Ps. 32:1. It then introduces December 30: the Holy Martyr Anysia of Thessalonica (d. 304) and Venerable Zoticus the Priest, Nourisher of the Poor and Leper-caregiver (*приставленаго нищимъ и трудоватыхъ кормителя*), noting that the service of St. Melania is transferred and combined with St. Anysia on account of the Apodosis of the Nativity. Finally, it records December 31: St. Melania the Roman and the Apodosis of the Nativity of Christ (*Отдаяніе праздника Рожества Христова*), opening Great Vespers with 4 festal stichera in Tone 2.

[^340]: Leaf p291 details the complete rubrics for December 31, the Apodosis of the Feast of the Nativity of Christ (*Отдаяніе праздника Рожества Христова*). Great Vespers features 6 festal stichera on *Lord, I have cried* (Tone 2: *Come, let us rejoice in the Lord* / *Пріидите, возрадуемся Господеви*), concluding with the famous Doxastikon of the Nun Cassia (*When Augustus reigned alone upon earth* / *Августу единовластвующу на земли*). Matins prescribes 3 repetitions of the festal Troparion, festal Sessional hymns, and the two Nativity Canons of St. Cosmas of Maiuma and St. John of Damascus (first canon to 8, second canon to 6; Katavasia: *Christ is born*, sung by both choirs together). Biblical Odes and Magnificat are omitted; festal Exapostilarion twice; Praises on 4 in Tone 4 (*Rejoice, ye righteous*); Great Doxology; and the Divine Liturgy with the 9th Odes of both canons (to 8), festal Troparion and Kontakion, Prokeimenon from LXX Ps. 65:4, 1, Alleluia from LXX Ps. 88:6 and Ps. 18:3, the daily Epistle and Gospel, and the festal Communion Hymn.

[^341]: Leaf p292 begins abruptly mid-service at Matins of January 1 (Circumcision of Christ and Feast of St. Basil the Great) with the Kontakion of St. Basil in Tone 4 (*Thou hast appeared as an unshakable foundation of the Church, dispensing an undeprived wealth unto earthborn men, sealing it with thy teachings, O venerable Basil, revealer of heaven!* / *Явися степень недвижима церкви...*). The preceding rubrics for January 1—including Vespers, the Festal Troparia, Kathismata, and Canons through Ode 6—are absent between fol. 151v (leaf p291, conclusion of Dec. 31) and fol. 152r (leaf p292), demonstrating a physical folio lacuna in the codex prior to the archival ink foliation. Matins continues with Ode 9 (omitting the Magnificat), Praises on 4 (3 festal Prosomoion and saint's sticheron in Tone 5; Glory in Tone 6 *Grace was poured out*, Both now *Descending unto the human race*), Great Doxology, dismissal, and anointing with holy oil. An important Typikon rule follows: an All-Night Vigil is observed only if January 1 falls on a Sunday; on weekdays no vigil is kept. The Divine Liturgy of St. Basil appoints Odes 3 and 6 from the canons, Prokeimenon from LXX Ps. 48:4, 2 (*My mouth shall speak wisdom*), Epistle from Colossians 2:8–12 (*Brethren, if any man take you by deceit through philosophy* / *Братіе, егда кто обокрадетъ вы философіею*), Alleluia from LXX Ps. 79:2, Gospel from Luke 2:20–21, 40–52, and Communion Hymn from LXX Ps. 148:1 and Ps. 111:6.

[^342]: Leaf p293 opens January 2, commemorating the Forefeast of the Theophany (*Предпразденство Просвѣщенія*) and St. Sylvester, Pope of Rome (d. 335). Vespers prescribes 3 stichera of the Forefeast in Tone 4 and 3 of the saint in Tone 8; Glory, Both now in Tone 6 (*He goes unto the Jordan* / *Идетъ къ Іордану*). The Aposticha features 3 idiomela of the Forefeast (*Coming unto the witness*, Tone 2 *Again Jesus*, and Tone 3) paired with the fixed Theophany refrains from LXX Ps. 41:7 (*Therefore will I remember Thee from the land of Jordan*) and LXX Ps. 76:17 (*The waters saw Thee, O God, the waters saw Thee*). The codex notes that these two psalm verses are chanted continuously at Vespers and Matins throughout the entire forefeast until the Eve of Theophany (January 5). The leaf concludes with the full text of the Forefeast Troparion in Tone 4: *Prepare thyself, O Zebulun, and adorn thyself, O Naphtali! O Jordan river, stand; receive with leaping the Master coming to be baptized! Rejoice, O Adam with the first mother; hide not yourselves as in paradise of old; for seeing you naked He hath appeared, that He may clothe you in your primal vesture: Christ hath appeared...* (*Готовися, Завулоне, и украшайся, Нефѳалиме...*).

[^343]: Leaf p294 concludes the Forefeast Troparion (*...wishing to renew all creation!* / *вся тварь хотя обновити*). At Matins, the canon of the Forefeast is sung to 6, and that of St. Sylvester to 6. After Ode 3, the codex records the Kontakion of St. Sylvester in Tone 4, Podoben: *Thou hast appeared today* (*As an astronomer unto the inhabited earth, God who is in glory set thee forth through virtues, more than the morning star, O blessed one; for thou art our luminary, O our father Sylvester!* / *Яко звѣздочетецъ вселеннѣй...*). After Ode 6, it sets forth the Forefeast Kontakion in Tone 4, Podoben: *Thou hast appeared today* (*In the streams of Jordan today, the Lord having arrived cried aloud unto John: Fear not to baptize Me, for I come to save Adam the first-fashioned!* / *Въ стругахъ днесь Іорданскихъ пришедъ Господь Іоанну вопіяше...*). Praises and Aposticha follow the standard Forefeast order. The leaf then sets forth the rubrics for the Saturday before the Enlightenment [Theophany] (*Суббота предпросвѣщеніемъ*): Epistle from 1 Timothy 3:13–4:5 (*Child Timothy, they that have served well...*) and Gospel from Matthew 3:1–11.

[^344]: Leaf p295 outlines the rubrics for the Sunday before the Enlightenment [Theophany] (*Недѣля предпросвѣщеніемъ*): Prokeimenon from LXX Ps. 27:9, 1 (*O Lord, save Thy people*), Epistle from 2 Timothy 4:5–8 (*Child Timothy, be vigilant in all things*), Alleluia from LXX Ps. 66:2 (*God be gracious unto us*), and the Gospel from Mark 1:1–8 (*The beginning of the Gospel of Jesus Christ*). It then introduces January 3: the Holy Prophet Malachi (ca. 400 BC) and Holy Martyr Gordius of Caesarea (d. 320). Vespers features 6 stichera of the saints, the Forefeast Aposticha in Tone 4, and Troparia. Matins combines the Forefeast canon (to 6) with two canons of the saints (to 8). After Ode 3, the Horologion transcribes the Kontakion of St. Gordius in Tone 4, Podoben: *Thou hast appeared today* (*With all love today the Church celebrates thy memory, O Gordius, praising thee secretly, that for which thou hast suffered, O beauty of martyrs and boast unto all!* / *Всяка любовію днесь церковь твою память съвершаетъ, Гордіе...*).

[^345]: Leaf p296 concludes January 3 with the Liturgy lections for St. Gordius: Prokeimenon from LXX Ps. 63:11, 2 (*The righteous shall rejoice in the Lord*), Epistle from 2 Timothy 1:3–9, Alleluia from LXX Ps. 91:13, Gospel from Matthew 10:16–22 (*Behold, I send you forth as sheep in the midst of wolves*), and Communion Hymn from LXX Ps. 111:6. It then introduces January 4: the Synaxis of the Holy Seventy Apostles (*Съборъ святыхъ апостолъ 70*) and Venerable Theoctistus, Abbot of Cucomo in Sicily (d. 800; whose service is transferred to Compline). Vespers combines the Forefeast and the Apostles. Matins appoints canons of the Forefeast (to 6) and Apostles (to 6). After Ode 3, it preserves the full Kontakion of the Seventy Apostles in Tone 1: *As radiant stars, as companions of the Twelve, ye received the clear gifts of Christ, to shine forth by your preaching, and bringing an innumerable multitude from the nations unto the faith, O divine Apostles; therefore today we praise with faith your memory!* (*Звѣзды свѣтлыя, яко [дванадесяти] съпоспѣшници Христова...*).

[^346]: Leaf p297 opens January 5, the Eve of Theophany (*Предпразденьство Просвѣщенія* / *Навечеріе*), commemorating Hieromartyr Theopemptus, Bishop of Nicomedia, Martyr Theonas (d. 304), Venerable Gregory of Crete, and Venerable Syncletica of Alexandria (d. ca. 350). Vespers combines the Forefeast with the saints and sets forth the Forefeast Troparion in Tone 4: *The river Jordan once turned back before the mantle of Elisha, when Elijah was taken up; and the waters were divided hither and thither, and the watery way became dry land unto him, as a true type of baptism, whereby we traverse the flux of transient life: Christ hath appeared at the Jordan to sanctify the waters!* (*Возвращашеся иногда Іорданъ рѣка...*). Matins appoints canons of the Forefeast (to 6) and saints (to 4), the Kontakion *In the streams of Jordan*, and standard Forefeast praises. Uniquely, the Horologion specifies that the Royal Hours must not be attached or concatenated directly to Matins on this day.

[^347]: Leaf p298 details the ceremonial order of the Great Royal Hours on the Eve of Theophany (January 5). At the appointed hour, the klepalo is struck, the community enters the temple, and the first two Royal Hours (First and Third) are celebrated together. The Priest and Deacon vest in their liturgical vestments (*своя ризы* / *въ сварнзы*), opening with the solemn exclamation *Blessed is our God*. The First Royal Hour comprises Psalm 5 (*Give ear to my words, O Lord* [LXX Ps. 5:2]), Psalm 22 (*The Lord shepherds me* [LXX Ps. 22:1]), and Psalm 26 (*The Lord is my light and my savior* [LXX Ps. 26:1]). Following the Great Litany, the two choirs chant the special Theophany Royal Hour hymns alternately in Tone 8: Troparion *Today the nature of the waters is sanctified*, followed by Psalm refrains from LXX Ps. 41:7 (*Therefore will I remember Thee*) and LXX Ps. 76:17 (*The waters saw Thee, O God*) paired with the Troparion *As man didst Thou come to the river, O Christ King*, concluding with the Idiomelon Doxastikon in Tone 8.

[^348]: Leaf p299 concludes the First Royal Hour on the Eve of Theophany with its Scriptural lectionary: Prokeimenon from LXX Ps. 17:14, 2 (*The Lord thundered from heaven*), Prophecy of Isaiah 35:1–10 (*The wilderness and the solitary place shall be glad*), Epistle from Acts 13:25–32 (St. John the Baptist's testimony before the people), and the Gospel from Matthew 3:13–17 (the Baptism of the Lord). Following the litany, Troparion (*The river Jordan once turned back*), Trisagion, and Kontakion (*In the streams of Jordan*), the Third Royal Hour begins immediately with the Priest's blessing and the chanting of Psalm 28 (*Bring unto the Lord, O ye sons of God* [LXX Ps. 28:1]), Psalm 41 (*As the hart pants after the fountains of water* [LXX Ps. 41:2]), and Psalm 50 (*Have mercy upon me, O God* [LXX Ps. 50:3]).

[^349]: Leaf p300 details the choral and liturgical structure of the Third Royal Hour for the Eve of Theophany. Following the Great Litany, the First Choir sings the Troparion in Tone 8 (*The right hand of the Forerunner* / *Десница Предтечева*), repeated by the Second Choir; then the Psalm verses from LXX Ps. 41:7 (*Therefore will I remember Thee*) and LXX Ps. 76:17 (*The waters saw Thee, O God*) are paired with the Troparion in Tone 4 (*Our God is a Trinity, He hath revealed Himself today indivisibly* / *Троица Богъ нашъ*). Glory, Both now in Tone 5 appoints *Coming in the flesh unto the Jordan, O Lord* (*Приходяй плотію ко Іордану, Господи*). The lectionary comprises the Prokeimenon from LXX Ps. 76:17 and Ps. 5:2 (*The waters saw Thee, O God*), Prophecy of Isaiah 1:16–20 (*Wash you, make you clean*), Epistle from Acts 19:1–8 (Paul at Ephesus baptizing in the name of the Lord Jesus), and Gospel from Mark 1:1–8. The Third Hour concludes with the litany, the Troparion *The river Jordan once turned back*, Trisagion, Kontakion *In the streams of Jordan*, 12 repetitions of *Lord, have mercy*, and the dismissal.

[^350]: Leaf p301 details the Sixth Royal Hour (*шестый часъ*) on the Eve of Theophany (January 5). At the 4th hour of the day (mid-morning), two tolls of the semantron signal the assembly.
