The priest and deacon vest in full sacred vestments to celebrate the Sixth and Ninth Royal Hours together. Following the opening blessing, Trisagion, and introductory prayers, the Sixth Hour appoints Psalm 73 (*O God, why hast Thou cast us off unto the end?* [LXX Ps. 73:1]), Psalm 76 (*With my voice have I cried unto God* [LXX Ps. 76:2]), and Psalm 90 (*He that dwells in the help of the Most High* [LXX Ps. 90:1]). Following the Great Litany, the two choirs alternately chant the appointed Theophany Sixth Hour troparia: Tone 8 *Thus says the Lord unto John* (*Си глаголетъ Господь къ Іоанну: Пророче, прииди, крести Мене*); then with the refrain from LXX Ps. 41:7 (*Therefore will I remember Thee*), the Tone 6 Troparion *Today the prophetic scripture is fulfilled* (*Днесь пророческое сбывается писаніе*); concluding with the Tone 5 Doxastikon *What turnest thou back, O Jordan, thy waters?* (*Что возвращаешися, Іордане*), sung by both choirs together. The lectionary comprises the Prokeimenon from LXX Ps. 28:3, 1 (*The voice of the Lord is upon the waters*), and the Prophecy of Isaiah 55:1–13 (*Ho, everyone that thirsts, come ye to the waters*).

[^351]: Leaf p302 concludes the lections of the Sixth Royal Hour: the Epistle of Great Saturday (Romans 6:3–11, St. Paul's fundamental discourse on baptism into Christ's death and resurrection) and the Gospel from Matthew 3:13–17 (*Then comes Jesus from Galilee to Jordan unto John, to be baptized of Him*). Following the Litany of Supplication, the Forefeast Troparion *The river Jordan once turned back*, the verse *Let Thy tender mercies speedily prevent us* (LXX Ps. 78:8), and the Kontakion *In the streams of Jordan*, the leaf introduces the Ninth Royal Hour (*девятый часъ*). After a brief pause, the Ninth Hour opens with Psalm 92 (*The Lord reigns, He is clothed with majesty* [LXX Ps. 92:1]), Psalm 113 (*In the exodus of Israel out of Egypt* [LXX Ps. 113:1]), and Psalm 85 (*Bow down Thine ear, O Lord, and hear me* [LXX Ps. 85:1]). The choral interchange features the Tone 7 Troparion *Terror was it to behold the Creator of heaven and earth* (*Ужасъ бѣ видѣти Творца небесе и земли*), paired with the refrains from LXX Ps. 41:7 and Ps. 76:17 and the Tone 2 Troparion *When the Forerunner came unto Him* (*Егда прииде къ нему Предтеча*).

[^352]: Leaf p303 concludes the Ninth Royal Hour and the Typika (*Изобразительныя*) on the Eve of Theophany. The choral climax of the Ninth Hour features the Tone 5 Doxastikon *Thy hand which touched the immaculate head of the Master* (*Руку твою прикоснувый пречистѣй версѣ Владычнѣ*). The lectionary comprises the Prokeimenon from LXX Ps. 26:1 (*The Lord is my light and my savior*), the Prophecy of Isaiah 49:8–15 (*In an acceptable time have I heard thee*), the Epistle from Titus 2:11–14 and 3:4–7, and the Gospel from Luke 3:1–18. Following the prayer of Azariah (*Deliver us not up utterly for Thy name's sake* [Dan. 3:34]), Trisagion, and Kontakion, the complete Typika service is celebrated: Psalm 102 (*Bless the Lord, O my soul*), Psalm 145 (*Praise the Lord, O my soul*), the Beatitudes recited simply without chant (*просто поемъ книги*), the penitential prayer of remission (*Ослаби, остави, прости, Боже*), the Kontakion, 40 repetitions of *Lord, have mercy*, and the dismissal. At the 10th hour of the day (late afternoon), the great klepalo summons the community for Great Vespers conjoined with the Divine Liturgy of St. Basil the Great.

[^353]: Leaf p304 sets forth Great Vespers conjoined with the Divine Liturgy of St. Basil the Great on the Eve of Theophany (January 5). Following the opening blessing, Trisagion, and Psalm 103 (*Bless the Lord, O my soul* [LXX Ps. 103:1]), the Great Litany is intoned. At *Lord, I have cried*, while the deacon censes, the priest prepares the Proskomide at the Table of Oblation. Eight stichera are chanted (4 festal idiomela repeated, alongside the saint's stichera), culminating in the famous Tone 2 Doxastikon *Thou bowedst Thy head unto the Forerunner* (*Преклонилъ еси главу Предтечи*). The Little Entrance is made with the Gospel book, followed by the daily Prokeimenon and the prescribed Old Testament lections (the 13 Paremias of Theophany). Following the Paremias and Little Litany, the priest recites the Trisagion prayers, the Trisagion is chanted, and the Divine Liturgy lections are proclaimed: Prokeimenon from LXX Ps. 26:1 (*The Lord is my light*), Epistle from 1 Corinthians 9:19 (*For though I be free from all men...*), Alleluia from LXX Ps. 44:2 (*My heart hath poured forth a good word*), and Gospel from Luke 3:1–18. St. Basil's Liturgy is celebrated according to its order. At the conclusion of the Liturgy, the rubrics record the solemn preparation for the Great Blessing of Waters: the priest places the Holy Cross upon a vessel/dish upon his head, accompanied by candles and censers, and processes to the water font.

[^354]: Leaf p305 details the Great Blessing of Waters (*Великое освященіе воды*) on the Eve of Theophany. The clergy process to the baptismal font or water vessel preceded by candles and incense, singing the Tone 8 Troparia of St. Sophronius of Jerusalem (*The voice of the Lord is upon the waters*, *Today the nature of the waters is sanctified*, and *Thus says the Lord unto John*). The Scriptural lectionary features the three Isaiah Prophecies (Isa. 35:1–10; 55:1–13; 12:3–6), Prokeimenon from LXX Ps. 26:1, Epistle from 1 Corinthians 10:1–4 (the typological crossing of the Red Sea as baptism into Moses), Alleluia from LXX Ps. 28:3, 1, and the Gospel from Mark 1:9–11 (the Baptism of Jesus). The deacon intones the solemn consecratory litany for the sanctification of the waters with its special petitions (*яже о священныхъ*). While the deacon chants the litany, the priest recites the silent prayer of humble self-abasement (*Господи Іисусе Христе, Единородный Сыне*); then, at the deacon's concluding cue, the priest intones aloud the majestic consecratory prayer of St. Sophronius: *Great art Thou, O Lord, and marvelous are Thy works* (*Велій еси, Господи, и чудна дѣла Твоя*). A precise rubrical rubric is provided for when a priest serves without a deacon: he recites the secret prayer while the Old Testament lections are being read, so that he is ready to begin the great audible consecration immediately after the litany.

[^355]: Leaf p306 records the core hieratic moment of the Great Blessing of Waters on Theophany Eve: the solemn recitation of the great prayer of St. Sophronius (*Great art Thou, O Lord, and marvelous are Thy works* / *Велій еси, Господи, и чудна дѣла Твоя*), the prayer of the bowing of heads (*Incline Thine ear, O Lord* / *Приклони, Господи, ухо Твое*), and the threefold blessing and vertical immersion of the Precious Cross into the water. As the Cross is immersed, the Festal Troparion in Tone 1 is sung in full: *When Thou wast baptized in the Jordan, O Lord, the worship of the Trinity was made manifest: for the voice of the Father bore witness unto Thee, naming Thee His beloved Son, and the Spirit in the form of a dove confirmed the certainty of the word: O Christ God, Who hast appeared and enlightened the world, glory to Thee!* (*Во Іорданѣ крещающути ся, Господи, Троическое явися поклоненіе...*). As the faithful are sprinkled with the newly sanctified Jordan water, the Troparion is chanted thrice. The clergy and people process back into the temple singing the Tone 6 Doxastikon *Let us praise, ye faithful* (*Воспоимъ, вѣрніи, еже о насъ Божія благодѣянія величество*) and Psalm 33 (*I will bless the Lord at all times* [LXX Ps. 33:2]).

[^356]: Leaf p307 provides liturgical and dietary regulations for the Eve of Theophany. Following the Great Blessing of Waters, antidoron (*преломленіе*) is distributed, the full dismissal is given, the festal Troparion and Kontakion are sung, and the community partakes of a fasting meal seasoned with olive oil (*древяное масло*), strictly abstaining from dairy and eggs (*сыръ и яйца*). A crucial Typikon statute governs the calendar collision when January 5 falls on a Saturday or a Sunday: the strict Paramony fast is relaxed (*поста нѣсть*), the Liturgy of St. John Chrysostom is celebrated in the morning at the normal hour, and the faithful consume blessed bread and a cup of wine in the church to sustain themselves. On such years, the Divine Liturgy of St. Basil the Great is transferred to the feast day itself (January 6). In the afternoon, the semantron is struck for Great Vespers (celebrated independently of Liturgy); if on Saturday evening, the first Kathisma (*Blessed is the man* [LXX Ps. 1]) is chanted, followed by 8 festal stichera on *Lord, I have cried*, the Doxastikon *Thou bowedst Thy head*, and the Little Entrance with the Gospel.

[^357]: Leaf p308 continues the rubrics for Great Vespers when Theophany Eve falls on a Saturday or Sunday. After the entrance and Paremias, the Little Litany is intoned, followed by the Prokeimenon from LXX Ps. 26:1. The Trisagion hymn (*Святый Боже*) is explicitly omitted because the Divine Liturgy is not joined to Vespers on this day (*не совершеныя ради литургіа*). The Epistle (1 Cor. 9:19) and Gospel are read immediately at Vespers, followed by the Litany of Supplication. The clergy then process to the water vessel chanting *The voice of the Lord is upon the waters*, performing the Great Blessing of Waters in its complete order (Paremias, Epistle, Gospel, prayer, immersion of the Cross, and sprinkling). Returning to the temple, they chant *Let us praise, ye faithful*, the Augmented Litany (*Помилуй насъ, Боже*), and the dismissal, followed by a meal with oil. The leaf then introduces the rubrics for when the Feast of Theophany itself (January 6) coincides with a Sunday (*въ неделю*).

[^358]: Leaf p309 provides instructions for the coincidence of Theophany with Sunday (*Недѣля*). In striking contrast to standard Sunday Typikon rules where the Resurrectional octoechos elements dominate, on Theophany the Resurrectional material is almost entirely suppressed in favor of the Despotic Feast (*на все праздниково*): no Resurrectional stichera, hypakoai, gradual hymns (*степенны*), Psalm 118 (*Блажени непорочніи*), Resurrectional Gospel, nor *Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ* (*Воскресеніе Христово видѣвше*) are sung; only three Psalter kathismata are chanted, and the faithful simply venerate the Gospel book. At the 2nd hour of the night (the opening of the All-Night Vigil), Great Compline (*павечерница*) is chanted. Following the Minor Doxology (*Glory to God in the highest*), five festal idiomela in Tone 4 are sung, with the Tone 8 Doxastikon *O Lord, wishing to fulfill what Thou hadst ordained* (*Господи, исполнити хотя*) and Theotokion *Today creation is enlightened*. The community then processes into the nave for Litiya (*Литія*), chanting the Tone 2 idiomela (*In the river Jordan*, *Today Christ comes to be baptized*, and *What ailed thee, O sea*) with their fixed psalm refrains (LXX Ps. 76:17 and Ps. 113:3, 5), concluding with the Tone 6 Doxastikon *He that clothes Himself with light as with a garment*, the blessing of the five loaves, wheat, wine, and oil (*благословеніе хлѣбомъ*), and the festal Troparion thrice.

[^359]: Leaf p310 details the structure of festal Matins (*Утреня*) for the Feast of the Holy Theophany (January 6). Following the Anavathmoi (Tone 4, 1st Antiphon *From my youth*), the Matins Prokeimenon is proclaimed from LXX Ps. 113:3, 5 (*The sea saw it and fled*), followed by the Matins Gospel, Psalm 50, and the festal Doxastikon. Two Theophany Canons in Tone 2 are appointed: the first by St. Cosmas of Maiuma (*Deep beneath the wave* / *Глубины открылъ есть дно*) and the second by St. John of Damascus (*The Lord mighty in battle* / *Шествуетъ сушу чуждую*), with the heirmoi repeated twice, troparia to 4, and the katavasia sung by both choirs together. After Ode 3, the Horologion transcribes the complete text of the ancient Tone 8 Hypakoe (*Егда явленіемъ Твоимъ просвѣтилъ еси всяческая*), followed by prescribed homilies from St. Gregory of Nazianzus (the Theologian) and Pseudo-Dionysius the Areopagite. After Ode 6, it sets forth the renowned Theophany Kontakion in Tone 4 by St. Romanos the Melodist (*Явился еси днесь вселеннѣй*). At Ode 9, the Magnificat (*Честнѣйшую*) is replaced by the festal Megalynaria and 9th Ode heirmoi; the Exapostilarion *Our Savior hath appeared* (*Явися Спасъ нашъ благодать и истина*) is sung thrice; and the Praises (*Хвалитехъ*) open on 4 with 3 idiomela in Tone 1.

[^360]: On leaf p311, the Great Blessing of Waters (Величественное освященіе водъ) is performed on the feast of Theophany at the water springs or cisterns following Matins and the dismissal. The three Old Testament lections (пареміи) read over the water are from the Prophet Isaiah (Isa. 35:1–10; Isa. 55:1–13; Isa. 12:3–6).

[^361]: Epistle reading at the Great Blessing of Waters on Theophany: 1 Cor. 10:1–4. The incipit *бра̃е, не велю* corresponds to *братіе, не хощу васъ не вѣдѣти* ("Brethren, I would not that ye should be ignorant"). The Great Prayer of Sanctification begins here with the incipit *Славимъ Тя, Владыко* ("We glorify Thee, O Master, Lover of mankind"), a variant opening of the great consecratory prayer attributed to St. Sophronius of Jerusalem.

[^362]: The festal antiphons of the Divine Liturgy of Theophany: Antiphon 1 is drawn from Psalm 113 [LXX 113:1–3] with the refrain *Through the prayers of the Mother of God, O Savior, save us*; Antiphon 2 is drawn from Psalm 114 [LXX 114:1–3] with the festal refrain *Save us, O Son of God, Who wast baptized in the Jordan, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia*.

[^363]: The Entrance hymn of the Liturgy of Theophany combines LXX Ps. 117:26 and Ps. 117:27 (*Blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord; God is the Lord, and hath appeared unto us*). In place of the Trisagion (*Святый Боже*), the baptismal hymn from Galatians 3:27 is sung: *As many of you as have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ. Alleluia*. The Kinonikon (Communion verse) is Titus 2:11.

[^364]: January 7: Synaxis of the Holy Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John (Соборъ святаго славнаго пророка, предтечи и крестителя Іоанна). The Great Prokeimenon at Vespers is Psalm 113:11 (*Our God is in heaven and on earth: He hath done whatsoever He pleased*). The Kontakion sung after Ode 6 is the Tone 6 Kontakion of the Synaxis (*Плотскаго Ти, Христе, пришествія убоявся Іорданъ...*).

[^365]: Liturgy of the Synaxis of the Forerunner: the Epistle lection from Acts 19:1–8 is appointed for the 6th Week of Pascha (*в па̃. ѕ҃. н҃е* = въ память 6-я недѣли, i.e. Acts 19:1–8); the Gospel is John 1:29–34; the Kinonikon is Psalm 111:6 (*In everlasting memory shall the righteous be*). Rubrics follow regarding the concurrence of the feast with a Sunday.

[^366]: Rubrics for Saturday and Sunday after Theophany: Saturday Epistle Eph. 6:10–17, Gospel Mt. 4:1–11; Sunday after Theophany Prokeimenon Psalm 32:22, Gospel Mt. 4:12–17. Followed by a liturgical rubrical directive (*сказъ*) regulating the calculation of variable Lucan/Matthean lections based on the day of the month. January 8 commemorates Ven. Domnica, Ven. George the Chozebite (in the text written as *Єфеѡрьꙗ/Геѡргіа*), and St. Emilian the Confessor.

[^367]: January 9: Commemoration of the holy martyr Polyeuctus and holy father Marcian (written as *маркелїна*). January 10: Commemoration of our father among the saints Gregory of Nyssa, St. Dometian of Melitene, and Ven. Marcian of Constantinople. The Sessional Hymn after Ode 3 is set to the model *Ликъ ангельскій* ("The angelic choir"). The Prokeimenon at Liturgy is Psalm 149:5 (*Восхвалятся преподобніи въ славѣ*).

[^368]: January 11: Commemoration of our venerable father Theodosius the Cenobiarch (Преподобнаго отца нашего Ѳеодосія Великаго, общаго житія начальника). At Vespers, three lections common to venerable monastics are appointed (Wisdom of Solomon 5:15–6:3; 3:1–9; 4:7–15). The Matins Gospel is Luke 6:17–23. The Katavasia is *Глубины откры подошву* (Heirmos of the Theophany Canon).

[^369]: January 12 commemorates the Holy Martyr Tatiana (Святыя мученицы Татіаны); January 13 commemorates Martyrs Hermylus and Stratonicus (Ермила и Стратоника); January 14 is the Apodosis (Apodosis / отданіе) of the Holy Theophany, combined with the commemoration of the Holy Fathers slain in Sinai and Raithu (преподобныхъ отцевъ въ Синаи и Раиѳѣ избіенныхъ) and St. Sava of Serbia.

[^370]: On leaf p321, the service of the venerable fathers slain in Sinai and Raithu (преподобныхъ отецъ въ Синаи и Раиѳѣ избіенныхъ) is sung in conjunction with St. Sava of Serbia. The Kontakion after Ode 3 is Tone 2 (*Ѡ мира сего мятежа обѣжасте...*). The Prokeimenon at Liturgy is Psalm 67:36 (*Marvelous is God in His saints*). Following this on January 14 begins the Apodosis (Apodosis / отданіе) of Holy Theophany, celebrating exclusively the festal hymns of Theophany at Vespers.

[^371]: On leaf p322, the full order for the Apodosis of Theophany (January 14) is set forth for Vespers, Matins, and Divine Liturgy, sung entirely from the feast without the Kathisma or lections at Vespers, and with both festal canons at Matins and Liturgy. The Communion verse (Kinonikon) is Titus 2:11 (*The grace of God that brings salvation hath appeared unto all men*).

[^372]: January 15: Commemoration of our venerable fathers Paul of Thebes (Павелъ Ѳивейскій) and John the Hut-Dweller (Іоаннъ Кущникъ / Calabytes). Beginning on this day, the regular winter Psalter allocation resumes following the conclusion of Theophany post-feasts: three Kathismata are chanted at Matins, and Kathisma 18 (*Къ Господу внегда скорбѣти*, Psalms 119–133) is chanted at daily Vespers. The Kontakion of St. Paul of Thebes after Ode 3 is sung to the model *Дѣва днесь* ("Today the Virgin").

[^373]: On leaf p324, a significant structural rubric decrees that from January 15 onward, the Mid-Hours (междучасія / Mesoria) are sung in the church, and the Supplicatory Canon (Paraklesis) to the Mother of God is added at Compline. January 16 commemorates the Veneration of the Precious Chains of the Holy Apostle Peter (Поклоненіе честныхъ веригъ святаго и всехвальнаго апостола Петра).

[^374]: At the Liturgy for the Chains of the Apostle Peter (January 16), the Epistle is from Acts 12:1–11 (St. Peter miraculously released from chains by an angel), the Prokeimenon is Psalm 18:5 (*Their sound is gone out into all the earth*), and the Gospel is the 11th Matins Resurrection Gospel (John 21:15–25, Christ's charge to Peter to shepherd His flock). January 17 opens the commemoration of our venerable father Antony the Great (Антоній Великій).

[^375]: January 17: Commemoration of our venerable and God-bearing father Antony the Great (Антоній Великій), celebrated with an All-Night Vigil (*всенощное*). At Great Vespers, Kathisma 1 (*Blessed is the man*) is chanted; the Stichera on *Lord, I have cried* are sung to the model *Called from on high* (Званному свыше); the three Old Testament lections are common to venerable fathers (Wisdom 5:15–6:3; 3:1–9; 4:7–15); and the blessing of loaves (Artoklasia) is celebrated.

[^376]: Matins and Liturgy of St. Antony the Great: the Polyeleos (*Многомилостивый*) is chanted, followed by the Gospel from Luke 6:17–23 and the Supplicatory Canon to the Mother of God (*Многими одержимь напастьми*). After the dismissal of Matins, the ancient rubric directs the priest to anoint the brethren with holy oil taken from the vigil lamp of the saint (*и даетъ святое масло братіи от кандила святаго*). The Liturgy Epistle is Galatians 5:22–6:2.

[^377]: January 18: Commemoration of our fathers among the saints, Archbishops of Alexandria, Athanasius the Great and Cyril (Аѳанасій и Кирилъ Александрійскіи). The Tone 4 Kontakion is *Священноначальницы велицыи благочестія...* ("Great hierarchs of piety..."). The Epistle is Hebrews 13:17–21 (appointed for Dec. 6 / St. Nicholas); Gospel is Matthew 5:14–19. January 19 commemorates our venerable father Macarius of Egypt (Макарій Египтянинъ).

[^378]: January 19: Commemoration of our venerable father Macarius of Egypt (Макарій Египтянинъ). The Kontakion after Ode 6 is Tone 4 (*Блаженную жизнь скончавъ въ житіи...*). January 20 opens the commemoration of our venerable and God-bearing father Euthymius the Great (Евѳимій Великій), where an All-Night Vigil is observed at the discretion of the superior (*аще хощеть настоятель / предстоятель*). The Stichera on *Lord, I have cried* are set to the model *O all-praised martyrs* (Прехвальніи мученицы).

[^379]: January 20: Order of Vespers and Matins for St. Euthymius the Great. At Vespers, the Tone 4 Troparion is *Веселися, пустыня нераждающая...* ("Be glad, O barren desert that bearest not...", based on Isaiah 54:1 / Galatians 4:27). At Matins, 3 Kathismata and the Polyeleos are sung, accompanied by the Canon of the Mother of God (*Holding fast amidst many temptations*) and the canon of the saint to 8. The Tone 8 Kontakion after Ode 6 is *Въ честнѣмъ ти рождествѣ радость тварь обрѣте...* ("In thy precious nativity the creation found joy...").

[^380]: On leaf p331, concluding the commemoration of St. Euthymius the Great (January 20) with the Ikos, Praises, and Liturgy readings. January 21 commemorates St. Maximus the Confessor (*Маѯимъ Исповѣдникъ*, misprinted as *иконвикъ* in Fiol's text), St. Neophytus (*Неофитъ*), and St. Eugene (*Евгеній*). The Tone 8 sticheron incipit rubric contains an aberrant typesetting form (*покыдно жество*, reflecting *О преславное чюдо* / *Подобенъ*).

[^381]: January 21: Full texts of both Kontakia are printed in the codex: first after Ode 3 to the Martyr Neophytus (*Богоявленію звѣзду вѣрѣ...*), and second after Ode 6 to St. Maximus the Confessor (*Свѣтъ трисіянный вселивыйся въ душу твою...*). At the Divine Liturgy, the Epistle is Hebrews 11:33–12:2 (appointed for All Saints) and Gospel is Matthew 7:24–8:4 (Saturday lection).

[^382]: January 22: Commemoration of the Holy Apostle Timothy (*Тимоѳей*) of the Seventy and the Venerable Martyr Anastasius the Persian (*Анастасій Персянинъ*). The joint Kontakion after Ode 6 is Tone 4 (*Божественнаго ученика и соузника Павлова, Тимоѳея...*). At the Divine Liturgy, the Epistle is 2 Timothy 1:3–9.

[^383]: Between leaf p333 (folio 172v) and leaf p334 (folio 173r), the Fiol codex transitions directly from the Liturgy of St. Timothy and St. Anastasius (January 22) to the Matins of St. Gregory the Theologian (January 25, *Григорій Богословъ*). The intermediate commemorations for January 23 (Hieromartyr Clement of Ancyra) and January 24 (Venerable Xenia of Rome), as well as the opening Vespers of St. Gregory, are omitted in this printing. The Kontakion after Ode 6 is the famous Tone 3 *Богословнымъ языкомъ своимъ...* sung to the model *Дѣва днесь*.

[^384]: January 26 commemorates Venerable Xenophon (*Ксенофонтъ*) and his family, along with the Translation of the Relics of St. Theodore the Studite (*Ѳеодоръ Студитъ*). The Kontakion is Tone 4 to the model *Явися днесь*. January 27 begins the major feast of the Translation of the Relics of St. John Chrysostom (*Пренесеніе мощей Іоанна Златоуста*). The rubrics explicitly cite the texts previously appointed for his main feast on November 13 (*по якоже 13*).

[^385]: January 27: Matins and Liturgy for the Translation of the Relics of St. John Chrysostom. The Kontakion after Ode 3 is Tone 1 (*Возвеселися тайно честная Церковь...*), while the Kontakion after Ode 6 is his primary feast Kontakion from November 13 (*Оть небесъ принялъ еси...*). The rubric specifies anointing the brethren with holy oil from the vigil lamp of the saint (*и дается святое масло, брано от кандила святаго*). January 28 opens the commemoration of our venerable father Ephrem the Syrian (*Ефремъ Сиринъ*).

[^386]: January 28: Order of Matins and Liturgy for St. Ephrem the Syrian. The Kontakion after Ode 6 is Tone 2 (*Часъ присно провидя испытанія...*). The Epistle is Galatians 5:22–6:2 and Gospel Matthew 11:27–30. January 29 commemorates the Translation of the Relics of the Holy Hieromartyr Ignatius the God-Bearer of Antioch (*Пренесеніе мощей Игнатія Богоносца*).

[^387]: January 30: Commemoration of the Three Holy Hierarchs (*Святіи три святители: Василій Великій, Григорій Богословъ, Іоаннъ Златоустъ*), together with the Hieromartyr Hippolytus, Pope of Rome (*Ипполитъ Римскій*). The rubric bears exceptional hymnographic witness by explicitly attributing the common service of the Three Hierarchs to its historical author, the 11th-century Byzantine hymnographer John Mauropous, Metropolitan of Euchaita (*въслѣдованіе... сътворено въсесвященымь митрополитомь Евхаїтскымь гдл Іѡанномъ*), while directing that St. Hippolytus's office be relegated to Compline.

[^388]: January 30: Matins order for the Three Holy Hierarchs. The famous Kontakion by John Mauropous is Tone 2 (*Священныя и боговещанныя проповѣдатели...*) sung to the model *Твердыя и боговѣщанныя*. During the distribution of holy oil after the dismissal, the choir chants stichera taken from the previous services of each hierarch earlier in the month: St. Basil (January 1), St. Gregory the Theologian (January 25), and St. John Chrysostom (January 27).

[^389]: January 31 concludes the month of January with the Holy Unmercenaries Cyrus and John (*Кѵръ и Іоаннъ*). The Kontakion after Ode 6 is Tone 3 (*Отъ божественныя благодати дарованіе чудесъ пріимше...*) sung to the model *Дѣва днесь*. At the base of leaf p340, a prominent decorative headpiece marks the transition into the Month of February (*Мѣсяць Февруарій*), specifying 28 days and opening with the commemoration of the Holy Martyr Tryphon (*Трифонъ*) and the Forefeast of the Meeting of the Lord in the Temple (*Срѣтеніе Господне*).

[^390]: February 1: Forefeast of the Meeting of the Lord in the Temple (*Срѣтеніе Господне*) and the Holy Martyr Tryphon (*Трифонъ*). The codex records the full texts of the ancient Forefeast Troparion (*Небесный ликъ небесныхъ ангелъ...*, Tone 1) and the Kontakion of St. Tryphon (*Мучительскаго прещенія многобожіе разрушилъ еси...*, Tone 8) sung to the model *Яко начатки* (here printed as *яко паткы*, preserving the archaic South Slavic/Old Church Slavonic translation of the Greek Ὡς ἀπαρχὰς τῆς φύсеως). Fiol's typesetter exhibits minor mechanical corruptions: *глапоють* for *глаголють* / *въспѣвають*, and *дїнкъ* for *мч҃нкъ*.

[^391]: February 2: The Great Feast of the Meeting of Our Lord Jesus Christ in the Temple (*Оусърѣтеніе Господа нашего Ісуса Христа*). Order of Little Vespers with the full Troparion (*Радуйся, обрадованная Богородице Дѣво...*, Tone 1) and Great Vespers with Old Testament lections from Exodus (Ex. 13:1–16), Isaiah (Is. 6:1–12), and Ezekiel (Ez. 43:27–44:4).

[^392]: February 2: Litiya and Matins for the Meeting of the Lord. The rubrics bear Studite liturgical witness, appointing the Heirmos twice and troparia on 4, followed by the Heirmos sung jointly by both choirs (*и паки ірмосъ оба лика вкупѣ*). The Matins Gospel is cross-referenced directly to the midday Liturgy reading from Luke (*отъ полу, емуже начало: Бѣ человѣкъ въ Іерусалимѣ*, Lk. 2:25–32).

[^393]: February 2: Concluding Matins and Divine Liturgy for the Meeting of the Lord. The Kontakion (*Иже утробу Дѣвичу освятивый...*, Tone 1) contains the archaic 15th-century Ruthenian/Slavic reading *но укрѣпи князя [люди / царя], егоже возлюбилъ еси* ("strengthen the prince whom Thou hast loved"). At Ode 9, the rubric directs that *Честнѣйшую* is omitted and candles are distributed to the brethren (*даются свѣщи братіи*), followed after the dismissal by a procession outside the monastery (*творимь исхоженіе изъ монастыря*). Divine Liturgy appointments include Hebrews 7:7–17 and Luke 2:22–40.

[^394]: February 3–5: Commemorations of the Holy and Righteous Symeon the God-Receiver (*Симеонъ Богопріимецъ*, Feb 3), Venerable Isidore of Pelusium (*Исидоръ Пелусіотъ*, Feb 4), and the Holy Martyr Agatha (*Агаѳія*, Feb 5). The full text of St. Isidore's Kontakion (*Яко веліе солнце Церкви...*, Tone 4) is printed in full. The text breaks off at line 19 of leaf p345 with the opening words of St. Agatha's Kontakion (*Язвила разумѣвъ...*).

[^395]: Major Codicological Lacuna: A massive quire loss occurs between leaf p345 (folio 175v) and leaf p346 (folio 176r). The surviving unique copy of Fiol's Horologion abruptly jumps from February 5 (St. Agatha) to May 7 (Apparition of the Sign of the Precious Cross over Jerusalem in 351 AD), omitting the remainder of February (Feb 6–28), the entire months of March and April, and the opening of May (May 1–6). Early ink foliation marks this leaf as folio `176` (with a later archivist's pencil query `241?`), reflecting continuous foliation of this defective physical volume. The leaf contains the full Troparion (*Креста Твоего образъ нынѣ паче солнца провозсія...*, Tone 8) and Kontakion (*Показавый Креста божественный образъ на небеси...*, Tone 4) of the Cross, transitioning directly into May 8 (St. John the Theologian and St. Arsenius the Great).

[^396]: May 8: Commemoration of the Holy Apostle and Evangelist John the Theologian (*Іоаннъ Богословъ*) and Venerable Arsenius the Great (*Арсеній Великій*). Prescribes Little Vespers, Great Vespers with lections, and a solemn Litiya with five idiomela (*Рѣка богословія*, *Прозяблъ чистоты*, etc.). At the conclusion of the Blessing of the Loaves, an appointed Patristic/Scriptural reading is taken specifically from the Apocalypse (*и чтеніе Апокалипціи*).

[^397]: May 8: Matins and Divine Liturgy for St. John the Theologian and St. Arsenius the Great. Includes the full Kontakion to St. Arsenius (*Отъ Рима возсіялъ еси, якоже солнце веліе...*, Tone 3). Epistle is 1 John 1:1–7 and Gospel John 19:25–27, 21:24–25. Physical leaf p348 exhibits a triangular loss at the top center of the parchment/paper leaf.

[^398]: May 9: Commemoration of the Holy Prophet Isaiah (*Исаія*), the Holy Martyr Christopher (*Христофоръ*), and the Translation of the Relics of St. Nicholas the Wonderworker (*Пренесеніе мощей святаго великаго отца нашего Николы* from Myra to Bari in 1087). Contains the full Kontakion to the Prophet Isaiah (*Пророчества даръ воспріемъ, пророче мучениче...*, Tone 2) sung to the model *Въ молитвахъ* (here noted as *яко паткы*).

[^399]: May 10–11: Commemoration of the Holy Apostle Simon the Zealot (*Симонъ Зилотъ*, May 10) with full Kontakion (*Павлу явися собесѣдникъ, апостоле...*, Tone 2, model *Къ вышнимъ*). May 11 opens the Dedication of the Imperial City of Constantinople (*Обновленіе Царяграда*) and the Hieromartyr Mocius the Presbyter (*Мокій презвитеръ*) with full Kontakion Tone 2 (*Вооруженъ щитомъ вѣрнымъ...*). The leaf concludes with the quire signature `ѕ҃ 4` (quaternion 6, leaf 4).

[^400]: May 11–15: Concluding words of the Kontakion to the Holy Hieromartyr Mocius the Presbyter (*Мокій презвитеръ*) from leaf p350. On May 12, the Horologion commemorates our holy fathers Epiphanius, Archbishop of Cyprus (*Епифаній Кипрскій*, d. 403), and Germanus, Patriarch of Constantinople (*Германъ Цареградскій*, d. 733/740); the rubric cites the common troparion of hierarchs *Боже отецъ нашихъ* ("O God of our fathers...", Tone 4, referencing the text as appointed on November 20) and provides the full text of the joint Kontakion in Tone 4 (*Священноначалныхъ чудное совкупленіе...*) sung to the model *Вознесыйся на крестъ*. May 13 commemorates the Holy Virgin Martyr Glyceria at Heraclea (*Гликерія*); May 14 commemorates the Holy Martyr Isidore of Chios (*Исидоръ*). May 15 begins the office of Venerable Pachomius the Great (*Пахомій Великій*, c. 292–348, founder of cenobitic monasticism in the Thebaid), appointing the office on 6 with the common monastic troparion *Слезъ твоихъ теченьми* ("With the streams of thy tears...", Tone 8, cf. September 3) and opening lines of his Kontakion in Tone 2 (*Светильникъ светелъ показался еси...*).

[^401]: May 15–21: Concluding strophes of the Kontakion of St. Pachomius the Great (*...и воздержаніемъ тѣло изнурилъ еси*) and Divine Liturgy appointments: Prokeimenon in Tone 7 (*Въсхвалятсѧ преподобніи*, LXX Ps. 149:5); Epistle lection 213 (Gal. 5:22–6:2); Alleluia (LXX Ps. 111:1); Gospel from Matthew, Thursday of the 3rd week (Mt. 11:27–30); Kinonikon (LXX Ps. 111:6). Commemorations for May 16–20: Venerable Theodore the Sanctified (*Ѳеодоръ Освященный*, disciple of Pachomius, May 16); Holy Apostle Andronicus of the Seventy (*Андроникъ*, May 17); Holy Martyr Theodotus of Ancyra (*Ѳеодотъ Анкирскій*) with Peter, Dionysius, and the seven virgin martyrs (May 18); Holy Hieromartyr Patricius, Bishop of Prusa (*Патрикій Прусскій*, May 19); and Holy Martyr Thalelaeus of Anazarbus (*Ѳалелей*, May 20). May 21 opens the major feast of the Holy Equals-to-the-Apostles Emperor Constantine the Great (*Константинъ*) and his mother Helena (*Елена*).

[^402]: May 21: Feast of the Holy Equals-to-the-Apostles Constantine and Helena. The codex records the full texts of the famous Troparion in Tone 8 (*Креста Твоего образъ на небеси видѣвъ...*) and Kontakion in Tone 3 (*Константинъ днесь съ матерію Еленою Крестъ являютъ...*) sung to the model *Дѣва днесь*. At the Divine Liturgy, the Prokeimenon is "Sing praises to our God, sing praises" (LXX Ps. 46:7) with verse "O clap your hands, all ye nations" (LXX Ps. 46:2), and the Epistle is Galatians 1:11–19 (lection 200). The rubrics preserve an important liturgical contingency: if May 21 falls on a Friday, the Epistle is read instead from the Acts of the Apostles (Acts 15:5–34, Thursday of the 5th week after Pascha).

[^403]: May 21–24: Concluding Divine Liturgy readings for Constantine and Helena: Alleluia in Tone 1 (LXX Ps. 88:20, 22), Gospel from John 10:1–9 (Thursday of the 5th week after Pascha), and Kinonikon LXX Ps. 111:6. Commemorations of Holy Martyr Basiliscus of Comana (*Василискъ*, May 22) and Venerable Michael the Confessor, Bishop of Synnada (*Михаилъ Синадскій*, May 23). May 24 contains the complete All-Night Vigil rubrics for Venerable Symeon the Stylite of the Wonderful Mountain (*Симеонъ столпникъ, иже на Дивнѣй горѣ*, 521–592): Great Vespers with Old Testament readings of the venerable from Wisdom (Wis. 3:1–9, 5:15–6:3, 4:7–15); Matins Polyeleos with Prokeimenon LXX Ps. 115:6, 3; Gospel from Luke 6:17–23; and Canon of the Theotokos (Tone 8, *Воду прошедъ / Многими одержимъ напастьми*) with the canon of the saint on 8.

[^404]: May 24–25: Full Kontakion in Tone 4 of St. Symeon the Stylite of the Wonderful Mountain (*Отъ младенства вку прилежа, отче...*) and Divine Liturgy appointments: Prokeimenon LXX Ps. 63:11, 2; Epistle Galatians 5:22–6:2; Alleluia LXX Ps. 91:13, 111:1; Gospel Matthew 11:27–30; Kinonikon LXX Ps. 111:6. May 25 begins the major feast of the Third Finding of the Precious Head of the Holy Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John (*Третье обрѣтеніе честныя главы Іоанна Предтечи*, c. 850): Great Vespers Kathisma 1, 3 Old Testament Lections as on the Beheading of the Baptist (August 29: Is. 40:1–5, 41:17–18, 45:8, 48:20–21; Mal. 3:1–3, 4:4–6; Wis. 4:7–15), Aposticha with verse LXX Ps. 91:13, and the opening of the Troparion in Tone 4 (*Яко божественное сокровище, сокровено въ земли...*).

[^405]: May 25–26: Concluding strophes of the Troparion of the Third Finding of the Head of the Forerunner (*...Христосъ откры ю намъ, пророче и Предтече*). Order of Matins with Polyeleos, Anabathmoi of Tone 4, Prokeimenon in Tone 4 (LXX Ps. 131:17, 1), Gospel from Matthew 11:2–15, and Kontakion in Tone 2 from February 24 (*Пророче Божій...*). Divine Liturgy readings: Prokeimenon LXX Ps. 63:11, 2; Epistle 2 Corinthians 4:6–15 (lection 176); Alleluia in Tone 4 (LXX Ps. 84:12); Gospel Matthew 11:2–15; Kinonikon LXX Ps. 111:6. May 26 opens the commemoration of the Holy Apostle Carpus of the Seventy (*Карпъ*, Bishop of Beroea in Thrace) with the common troparion of an apostle (Tone 3).

[^406]: May 26 – June 2: Full text of the Kontakion to Apostle Carpus in Tone 2 (*Яко святителя честна и стратотерпца крѣпка...*). Commemorations of Holy Hieromartyr Therapon of Sardis (*Ѳерапонтъ*, May 27); Venerable Nicetas, Archbishop of Chalcedon (*Никита Халкидонскій*, May 28); Holy Virgin Martyr Theodosia of Tyre (*Ѳеодосія*, May 29); Venerable Isaac, Hegumen of Dalmatos (*Исаакій Далматскій*, May 30); and Holy Martyr Hermeas of Comana (*Еремей*, May 31). Major liturgical calendar heading marking the Month of June (*Мѣсяць Іюнь иматъ дній 30; день иматъ часа 15, а нощь 9*). Commemorations for June 1: Holy Martyr Justin the Philosopher (*Іустинъ Философъ*) and another Justin; and June 2: our father among the saints Nicephorus the Confessor, Patriarch of Constantinople (*Никифоръ Цареградскій*, commemoration of the translation of his relics in 846), with the common troparion *Правило вѣрѣ* ("A rule of faith...", Tone 4, cf. Dec 6) and the opening of his Kontakion in Tone 4 (*Побѣдный вѣнецъ, о Никифоре...*) sung to the model *Вознесыйся*.

[^407]: June 2–8: Concluding words of the Kontakion to St. Nicephorus of Constantinople (*...яко святителя Христова и учителя*). Commemorations for June 3–7: Holy Martyr Lucillian (*Лукилліанъ*, June 3); our father among the saints Metrophanes, first Patriarch of Constantinople (*Митрофанъ*, d. 326) on June 4, with his full Kontakion in Tone 4 (*Отъ младенства явился еси со священными...*); Holy Hieromartyr Dorotheus, Bishop of Tyre (*Дороѳей*, June 5); Venerable Bessarion the Wonderworker and Venerable Hilarion the New, Hegumen of Dalmatos (*Виссаріонъ и Иларіонъ*, June 6); and Holy Hieromartyr Theodotus, Bishop of Ancyra (*Ѳеодотъ Анкирскій*, June 7). June 8 opens the feast of the Translation of the Relics of the Holy Great-Martyr Theodore Stratelates (*Ѳеодоръ Стратилатъ*, d. 319) with Vespers and Matins rubrics, citing the Troparion from February 8 (*Воинствословіемъ истиннымъ...*, Tone 4).

[^408]: June 8–11: Concluding Matins and Liturgy rubrics for Great-Martyr Theodore Stratelates (Kontakion *Мужествомъ души...*, Tone 2, and Liturgy readings as on February 8). June 9 commemorates our holy father Cyril, Patriarch of Alexandria (*Кириллъ Александрійскій*, d. 444, champion of the Council of Ephesus), with troparion *Православію наставниче* (Tone 8, cf. January 18) and full Kontakion in Tone 6 (*Бездноу ученій богословія источилъ еси...*) sung to the model *Иже о насъ исполнивъ смотреніе*. June 10 commemorates the Holy Hieromartyr Timothy, Bishop of Prusa (*Тимоѳей Прусскій*). June 11 opens the feast of the Holy Apostles Bartholomew (*Варѳоломей*) and Barnabas (*Варнава*) of the Seventy, with Vespers, Matins canons (Octoechos on 6, Apostles on 8), and the opening of the Kontakion to St. Barnabas in Tone 3 (*Христовъ бывъ еси истинный служитель...*) sung to the model *Дѣва днесь*.

[^409]: June 11–12: Concluding strophes of the Kontakion of Apostle Barnabas (*...сего ради твою божественную память, Варнаво, почитаемъ, славяще тя*), followed by the full Kontakion of Apostle Bartholomew in Tone 4 (*Явился еси веліе солнце церковное...*) sung to the model *Явился еси днесь*. Divine Liturgy readings for the Apostles: Prokeimenon in Tone 8 (LXX Ps. 18:5, 2); Epistle from Acts 11:19–30 (5th week after Pascha); Alleluia (LXX Ps. 88:6); Gospel Luke 10:16–21 (lection 51); Kinonikon LXX Ps. 18:5. June 12 commemorates our venerable fathers Onuphrius the Great (*Онуфрій Великій*) and Peter of Mount Athos (*Петръ Аѳонскій*): common monastic troparion *Боже отецъ нашихъ* (Tone 4) and full Kontakion to St. Peter of Athos in Tone 2 (*Удалилъ еси себе человѣческаго сожительства...*) sung to the model *Къ вышнимъ ищя*.

[^410]: On fol. 361, the Menaion commemorates the Holy Martyr Aquilina and St. Triphyllius of Leucosia on June 13, and opens the office for the Prophet Elisha and Patriarch Methodius on June 14.

[^411]: On fol. 362, the rubrics prescribe dual kontakia for St. Methodius (Tone 2, honoring his defense of the holy icons) and the Prophet Elisha (Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest..."), concluding with the commemoration of the Prophet Amos on June 15.

[^412]: On fol. 363, commemorations include St. Tychon of Amathus (June 16), the Persian Martyrs Manuel, Sabel, and Ismael (June 17), the Martyr Leontius (June 18), and the Apostle Jude, brother of the Lord (June 19), whose troparion refers to the common troparion for St. Symeon the Kinsman of the Lord on April 27.

[^413]: On fol. 364, the liturgical appointments conclude for the Apostle Jude, followed by St. Methodius of Patara (June 20), St. Julian of Tarsus (June 21), St. Eusebius of Samosata (June 22), St. Agrippina (June 23), and the vigil opening for the Nativity of St. John the Baptist (June 24).

[^414]: On fol. 365, the rubrics set forth the order of Small and Great Vespers for the feast of the Nativity of the Honorable Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John (June 24), including the troparion *Прро́че и҆ прѣдте́че* (Tone 4), Old Testament readings, Litiya, and the blessing of loaves.

[^415]: On fol. 366, Matins for the Nativity of the Forerunner continues with the Polyeleos, Megalynarion, the Gospel lection from Luke, and the two canons (one in Tone 6 and one in Tone 4 by St. John Damascene), followed by the Kontakion in Tone 2 (*Непло́ды днесь*).

[^416]: On fol. 367, the Liturgy for the Nativity of the Forerunner is concluded, followed by the commemoration of the Venerable Martyr Febronia on June 25 with her kontakion in Tone 4 (*И҆зъ мла́да все́ ѡ҆ста́вльши*).

[^417]: On fol. 368, the afterfeast of the Forerunner is concluded alongside St. Febronia, followed by the commemoration of St. David of Thessalonica (June 26) with his tree-ascetic kontakion in Tone 1 (*Са́дъ присноцвѣтꙋ́щь*), and St. Samson the Hospitable (June 27) with his kontakion in Tone 8 (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ врача́ прѣизрѧ́дна*).

[^418]: On fol. 369, St. Samson's kontakion concludes, followed by the feast of the Translation of the Relics of the Holy Unmercenaries Cyrus and John on June 28 with their troparion in Tone 1 and kontakion in Tone 2 (*Велї́кое враче́ство*).

[^419]: On fol. 370, the Divine Liturgy for Sts. Cyrus and John concludes, followed by the opening of the All-Night Vigil for the Holy Preeminent Apostles Peter and Paul on June 29, including Small and Great Vespers, the common troparion in Tone 4 (*И҆́же а҆по́столѡвъ прѣкопрѣсто́лници*), Litiya, Aposticha, and the patristic reading of the interpretation (*толкованїе*).

[^420]: On fol. 371, Matins and Divine Liturgy rubrics for the feast of the Holy Preeminent Apostles Peter and Paul (June 29) are set forth, including the Matins Gospel, the automelon kontakion in Tone 2 (*Твердыя и боговещанныя проповѣдателя*), the distribution of candles at Ode 9, the post-dismissal anointing with holy oil, and the Liturgy Epistle from 2 Corinthians (lection 193).

[^421]: On fol. 372, the Divine Liturgy for Sts. Peter and Paul concludes with Matthew 16:13–19, followed by the complete Vespers and Matins appointments for the Synaxis of the Twelve Holy Apostles (June 30), featuring their kontakion in Tone 2 (*Петра вѣрнїи камень вѣрѣ похвалимъ*).

[^422]: On fol. 373, the Divine Liturgy for the Twelve Apostles concludes, followed by the decorative headpiece opening the month of July (31 days). Commemorations include Sts. Cosmas and Damian (July 1) and the feast of the Deposition of the Precious Robe of the Most Holy Theotokos at Blachernae (July 2) with its troparion in Tone 8 (*Богородице приснодѣво*).

[^423]: On fol. 374, the feast of the Deposition of the Precious Robe concludes with its kontakion in Tone 4 (*Ѡдѣнїе всѣмъ вѣрнымъ*) and Liturgy lections, followed by the commemorations of St. Hyacinth (July 3) and St. Andrew, Archbishop of Crete (July 4), with his kontakion in Tone 2 (*Вострубивъ ясно*).

[^424]: On fol. 375, the kontakion of St. Andrew of Crete concludes, followed by the commemorations of Venerable Martha and St. Athanasius the Athonite, founder of the Great Lavra of Mount Athos (July 5), with All-Night Vigil rubrics and his troparion in Tone 3 (*Иже въ плоти житїю твоему*).

[^425]: On fol. 376, Matins and Liturgy for St. Athanasius of Athos conclude with his kontakion in Tone 4 (*Яко невещественныя существомъ зря*), followed by Venerable Sisoes the Great (July 6) with his kontakion (*Постникъ аггелъ на земли*), and Sts. Thomas and Acacius (July 7).

[^426]: On fol. 377, the commemoration of St. Acacius of the Ladder concludes, followed by the feast of the Holy Great-Martyr Procopius (July 8) with Vespers, Matins, his kontakion in Tone 2 (*Божественною ревностїю Христовою распалаемь*), and Liturgy rubrics.

[^427]: On fol. 378, the Liturgy for St. Procopius concludes, followed by Hieromartyr Pancratius of Taormina (July 9), the 45 Martyrs of Nicopolis (July 10), and the feast of the Holy Great-Martyr Euphemia the All-Praised (July 11), commemorating her miracle at the Council of Chalcedon, with her troparion in Tone 4 (*Жениха своего возлюбивши*) and kontakion in Tone 8 (*Подвигы странїя*).

[^428]: On fol. 379, the kontakion of St. Euphemia concludes with explicit reference to the 630 God-bearing Fathers of Chalcedon, followed by the Liturgy, the Martyrs Proclus and Hilary (July 12), and the Synaxis of the Holy Archangel Gabriel and St. Stephen the Sabbaite (July 13).

[^429]: On fol. 380, the Synaxis of Archangel Gabriel concludes with his kontakion in Tone 4 (*Бесплътнымъ слугамъ ты еси предстатель*), followed by the Holy Apostle Aquila (July 14) with his kontakion in Tone 4 (*Яко велїе солнце церкви*), and the feast of the Holy Martyrs Cyricus and Julitta alongside the Falling Asleep of the Holy Equal-to-the-Apostles, the Great Prince Volodymyr [Vladimir], named Basil, who baptized the Rus' Land (July 15).

[^430]: On fol. 381, the service of the Holy Martyrs Cyricus and Julitta concludes with the completion of their kontakion in Tone 3 and Liturgy lections (including 1 Cor. 13:11). July 16 commemorates the Hieromartyr Athenogenes alongside the solemn Synaxis of the Holy Fathers of the Six Ecumenical Councils, meticulously detailing the traditional patristic counts: Chalcedon (630), Nicaea I (318), Constantinople I (150), Ephesus (200), Constantinople II (167), and Constantinople III (170).

[^431]: On fol. 382, the feast of the Holy Fathers of the Ecumenical Councils continues with the troparion in Tone 8 (*Препрославленъ еси Христе Боже нашъ*), the famous dogmatic kontakion in Tone 8 (*Апостольское проповѣданїе и отеческая преданїя*), and complete liturgical rubrics for Matins and Divine Liturgy, prescribing the Epistle from Titus 3:8–15 (the standard lection for the Holy Fathers).

[^432]: On fol. 383, the Liturgy for the Holy Fathers concludes with Matthew 5:14–19, noting that the commemoration of St. Athenogenes is transferred at the ecclesiarch's discretion. Commemorations for July 17 follow: the Holy Great-Martyr Marina (Margaret) and the translation of the relics of St. Lazarus of Mount Galesios, featuring Marina's ancient kontakion in Tone 3 (*Марина днесь вражїю главу скрушаетъ*).

[^433]: On fol. 384, the commemorations for the second half of July proceed with the Martyr Emilian (July 18, Alleluia service), St. Macrina (sister of St. Basil the Great) and St. Dios the Wonderworker (July 19) with his kontakion in Tone 2 (*Мужествомъ души божественнѣ вооружився*), and the opening of the Great Feast of the Holy and Glorious Prophet Elijah (July 20), featuring his classical troparion in Tone 4 (*Во плоти ангелъ, пророковъ степень*).

[^434]: On fol. 385, the feast of the Prophet Elijah concludes with his troparion's ending, his kontakion in Tone 2 (*Пророче и прозорливче великихъ дѣлъ Бога нашего*), and complete Divine Liturgy rubrics (specifying James 5:10–20 and Luke 4:22–30). Commemorations for July 21 follow: Venerable John and Symeon the Fool-for-Christ and the Prophet Ezekiel, with the opening of the saints' kontakion in Tone 2 (*Христовою любовїю распалаема*).

[^435]: On fol. 386, the commemoration of Venerable John and Symeon concludes, followed by Holy Myrrh-bearer Mary Magdalene, Equal-to-the-Apostles, and the Hieromartyr Phocas (July 22), with her troparion in Tone 1 (*Христу, насъ ради отъ Дѣвы рождшемуся*) and the opening of her kontakion in Tone 3 to the podoben *Today the Virgin* (*Предстоящи, преславная, у креста Спасова*).

[^436]: On fol. 387, Mary Magdalene's kontakion concludes, followed by Liturgy rubrics, the Martyrs Trophimus and Theophilus (July 23, Alleluia), and the opening of the feast of the Holy Martyr Christina alongside the Holy Passion-bearing Princes Boris and Gleb (baptized Roman and David) on July 24, prescribing an All-Night Vigil with Old Testament lections and solemn Aposticha stichera.

[^437]: On fol. 388, Matins for St. Christina and the Holy Passion-bearers Boris and Gleb proceeds with the Polyeleos, the Matins Gospel from Luke, St. Christina's kontakion in Tone 4 (*Свѣтовидная голубица позналася еси*), and the opening of the kontakion of Sts. Boris and Gleb in Tone 3 to the podoben *Today the Virgin* (*Возсїя днесь преславная память ваю*).

[^438]: On fol. 389, the service of Sts. Boris and Gleb concludes with their Praises, an allusion to their status as the first-fruits of Rus' baptism (*Первоновокрѣщени*), and the Divine Liturgy lections (Romans 8:28–39 and John 15:17–16:2). Commemorations for July 25 follow: the Falling Asleep of St. Anna, mother of the Mother of God, alongside Holy Olympias the Deaconess and Venerable Eupraxia, with St. Anna's troparion in Tone 4 (*Жизнь рождшую во утробѣ носила еси*).

[^439]: On fol. 390, the feast of the Falling Asleep of St. Anna concludes with her troparion's ending, her kontakion in Tone 2 (*Прародителей Христовыхъ празднуемъ память*), and Divine Liturgy lections. Commemorations follow for the Hieromartyr Hermolaus and his companions (July 26, Alleluia), and the opening of the Great Feast of the Holy Great-Martyr and Unmercenary Healer Panteleimon (July 27) with his troparion in Tone 3 (*Страстотерпче Христовъ Пантелеимоне*).

[^440]: Physical leaf p391 concludes the troparion of the Holy Great-Martyr and Healer Panteleimon begun on physical leaf p390: "...Passion-bearer of Christ, Panteleimon, entreat the Merciful God [that He grant remission of sins unto our souls]."

[^441]: The 1491 Krakow print preserves an archaic Ruthenian recensional witness for the Kontakion of St. Panteleimon: *Подобникъ сый млтвнику [Милостивому], и исцѣленіемъ блгдть приемъ, страстотерпче, мучениче Христовъ...* diverging slightly from the later standard Synodal form (*Подражатель сый Милостиваго...*).

[^442]: The liturgical pericope references for St. Panteleimon's Liturgy correspond to: Epistle 2 Tim. 2:1–10 (lection 292 / Slavonic lection 25); Gospel John 15:17–16:2 (lection 52).

[^443]: July 28 commemorates the Holy Apostles of the Seventy: Prochorus, Nicanor, Timon, and Parmenas, chosen among the first seven deacons of the Church in Jerusalem (Acts 6:1–6).

[^444]: July 30 commemorates the Holy Apostles of the Seventy: Silas, Silvanus, Crescens, Epenetus, and Andronicus (Acts 15:22; 2 Cor. 1:19).

[^445]: Righteous Eudocimus of Cappadocia (9th c.) was renowned for his purity, justice, and uncorrupt relics.

[^446]: Physical leaf p393 records historic Ruthenian dietary rubrics for the opening of the Dormition Fast (*Успенський піст* / *Спасівка*): on July 31 Christians cease eating meat, and from August 1 strict fasting without fish is observed until the feast of the Falling Asleep of the Mother of God (August 15).

[^447]: August 1 marks the Procession of the Precious and Life-giving Cross of the Lord (*Происхожденіе Честнаго Креста*), instituted in Constantinople due to seasonal sicknesses in August, joined with the commemoration of the Seven Holy Maccabee Martyrs (Habim, Antonius, Gurias, Eleazar, Eusebonus, Alimus, Marcellus), their mother Solomonia, and their teacher Eleazar (2 Macc. 6–7). The rubric details the ritual extraction of the Cross from the treasury (*сосудохранилище* / *skeuophylakion*) to the Holy Table before Vespers.

[^448]: Internal cross-reference in the 1491 Horologion: *(see fol. 24)* refers to the Common of Martyrs located on physical leaf p48 (printed fol. 24).

[^449]: Physical leaf p395 reflects a codex folio transposition / misbinding in the 1491 printed exemplar. Leaves p395 and p396 contain the offices for the Apodosis of the Transfiguration and St. Maximus the Confessor (August 13) followed by August 8–12, while physical leaf p397 resumes the August 1 Matins order directly where physical leaf p394 left off. In strict compliance with MTS-1 and Rule 15, the text is transcribed and translated exactly as inked in physical binding order. Internal cross-reference *(see fol. 11)* points to physical leaf p22 (printed fol. 11).

[^450]: St. Maximus the Confessor (c. 580–662) is commemorated on August 13 (the transfer of his relics) along with his disciples Anastasius the Apocrisiarius and Anastasius the Monk.

[^451]: Internal cross-reference: *(see fol. 27)* refers to the Common Epistle lections on physical leaf p54 (printed fol. 27).

[^452]: The Holy Apostle Matthias was chosen by lot to replace Judas Iscariot among the Twelve (Acts 1:21–26).

[^453]: Physical leaf p397 bears a red heading cross (`+`) at the upper left margin, resuming the August 1 order of Matins for the Procession of the Precious Wood of the Cross immediately following the troparia cited at the bottom of physical leaf p394.

[^454]: The Sessional Hymn for the Maccabees draws upon Proverbs 9:1: "Wisdom hath builded her house, she hath hewn out her seven pillars."

[^455]: This rubric describes the solemn bringing forth of the Precious Wood of the Cross during the Great Doxology, when the priest vests, censes the altar and Cross, and bears it in procession into the nave for veneration.

[^456]: August 2 marks the Translation of the Relics of the Holy Protomartyr and Archdeacon Stephen from Jerusalem to Constantinople (c. 415).

[^457]: The Kontakion of St. Stephen highlights him as the first seed sown upon earth by the Heavenly Husbandman and the first to shed his blood for Christ.

[^458]: The Holy Seven Youths of Ephesus (Maximilian, Jamblicus, Martinian, John, Dionysius, Exacustodian, and Antoninus) were miraculously preserved in sleep in a cave for nearly two centuries (c. 250 to c. 446).

[^459]: August 5 marks the Forefeast of the Transfiguration of Our Lord Jesus Christ, paired with the commemoration of the Holy Martyr Eusignius of Antioch.

[^460]: The Troparion of the Forefeast of the Transfiguration directs the faithful to ascend Mount Tabor in spirit to behold the divine radiance of Christ.

[^461]: August 6 is the Great Feast of the Holy Transfiguration of Our Lord God and Savior Jesus Christ (*Преображеніе Господне*), celebrated with an All-Night Vigil.

[^462]: Physical leaf p401 continues the festive order for August 6, the Great Feast of the Holy Transfiguration of Our Lord God and Savior Jesus Christ (*Преображеніе Господне*), concluding Small Vespers and opening Great Vespers.

[^463]: The three Old Testament readings (*Paroimiai*) prescribed for the Transfiguration are: Exodus 24:12–18 (Moses ascents Mount Sinai to receive the tablets of stone); Exodus 33:11–23 (Moses beholds the glory of God in the cleft of the rock); and 3 Kings 19:3–16 (Elijah's vision of God on Mount Horeb in a still small voice).

[^464]: Physical leaf p402 directs reading from the homilies of St. John Chrysostom on the Gospel of Matthew (Homilies 56 and 57 on the Transfiguration), and designates the two canonical festive Canons: the first by St. Cosmas the Melodist (*Хоры Израильтестии*, Tone 4) and the second by St. John of Damascus (*Воду прошед*, Tone 8).

[^465]: The Kontakion of the Transfiguration (*На горѣ преобразился еси...*, Tone 7, Idiomelon / Automelon) encapsulates the theological purpose of the manifestation on Mount Tabor: that the disciples, beholding Christ crucified, might perceive His voluntary Passion and proclaim unto the world that He is truly the Radiance of the Father.

[^466]: Liturgical pericope references for the Divine Liturgy of the Transfiguration: Epistle 2 Peter 1:10–19 (Catholic lection 65); Gospel Matthew 17:1–9 (lection 70).

[^467]: Physical leaf p403 records the ancient Byzantine and monastic ritual of the Blessing of Grapes (*стафиліе* / σταφυλή) at the conclusion of the Divine Liturgy on August 6, recited following the distribution of the antidoron (*дора* / ἀнτίδωрон).

[^468]: Physical leaf p404 preserves the ancient Ruthenian recension of the *Prayer for the Blessing of Grapes* (*Молитва на благословеніе гроздія*), invoking God Who adorned the earth with flowers and ripens fruits through the dew, the gentle breeze, and the heat of the sun, that the tasting of these earthly fruits may lead unto spiritual joy and thanksgiving through the Blood and Body of Christ.

[^469]: Physical leaf p405 prescribes the strict monastic penance (*епитимія*) barring any brother or vinedresser who prematurely tastes grapes prior to August 6 from eating grapes for the entire remainder of August. It further specifies the dietary dispensation for grapes at the refectory thrice weekly at the midday meal.

[^470]: August 7 commemorates the Holy Venerable-Martyr Dometius of Persia and his two disciples (d. 363 under Emperor Julian the Apostate).

[^471]: Physical leaf p406 marks August 14: the Forefeast of the Falling Asleep of the Most Holy Theotokos, joined with the commemoration of the Holy Prophet Micah (one of the Twelve Minor Prophets, 8th c. BC).

[^472]: Physical leaf p407 reflects a physical signature misbinding in the 1491 Krakow exemplar. Folio 210 (leaves p407 and p408) preserves the conclusion of the ritual order for August 1 (Procession of the Precious Wood of the Life-Giving Cross and Small Blessing of Waters), which was initiated on physical leaves p394 and p397. Leaf p408 (the verso of folio 210) textually immediately precedes leaf p407 (the recto), as marked by the connecting rubrical cross (`+`). In strict compliance with Rule 15 and MTS-1, the codex text is transcribed and translated in exact physical leaf pagination order.

[^473]: The monastic ritual of lustrating the monastery with freshly blessed water and the Precious Cross on August 1: the priest in full vestments, accompanied by chanting brethren, processes throughout all monastic quarters—the Igumen's cell, refectory (*трапеза*), cellar (*келарня*), kitchen (*поварня*), bakery (*пекленица*), granary (*житница*), cellars (*погребы*), and all individual monks' cells.

[^474]: Liturgical pericope references for August 1 (Procession of the Cross and the Seven Maccabee Martyrs): Epistle 1 Cor. 1:18–24 (lection 125); Gospel John 19:6–11, 13–20, 25–28, 30–35 (lection 60). For the Maccabees: Epistle Hebrews 11:33–40 (lection 330 / to All Saints); Gospel Matthew 10:32–33, 37–38; 19:27–30 (lection 38).

[^475]: Physical leaf p408 records the rubric for bringing forth the Precious Cross from the altar onto a prepared table in the nave during the chanting of the Trisagion at the Great Doxology, followed by the Small Blessing of Waters and the solemn veneration of the Cross (*Поклоненіе честному древу*).

[^476]: Physical leaf p409 opens the commemoration for August 15: the Great Feast of the Falling Asleep (Dormition) of the Most Holy Lady Theotokos and Ever-Virgin Mary (*Успеніе Пресвятыя Богородицы*), celebrated with an All-Night Vigil.

[^477]: The Troparion of the Dormition (*Въ рождествѣ дѣвство сохранила еси...*, Tone 1) professes the central mystery of the feast: in giving birth She preserved Her virginity, and in falling asleep She did not forsake the world.

[^478]: Physical leaf p410 details the liturgical order for Dormition Matins, prescribing the two canonical festive Canons: the first by St. Cosmas of Maiuma (*Пособитницею*, Tone 1) and the second by St. John of Damascus (*Преукрашенная божественною славою*, Tone 4), along with a reading from the Life of the Mother of God by Symeon Metaphrastes (*Метафрастъ*).

[^479]: Physical leaf p411 continues the festal rubrics for August 15, the Great Feast of the Falling Asleep (Dormition) of the Most Holy Lady Theotokos (*Успеніе Пресвятыя Богородицы*), concluding Matins with the festive Kontakion (*Въ молитвахъ неусыпающую Богородицу...*, Tone 2, Automelon / Idiomelon).

[^480]: Liturgical pericope references for the Divine Liturgy of the Dormition: Epistle Philippians 2:5–11 (lection 240); Gospel Luke 10:38–42; 11:27–28 (lection 54); Communion Hymn Psalm 115:4 (*Чашу спасенія прииму...*).

[^481]: The rubric notes the 9-day celebration of the Dormition (August 15–23) and refers detailed afterfeast regulations (*междупразднество*) to the structural chapters (*главы*) of the Typikon.

[^482]: Physical leaf p412 marks August 16: the Translation of the Icon Not Made with Hands of Our Lord Jesus Christ (*Нерукотворенный Образъ*, the Holy Mandylion / *Святый Убрусъ*) from Edessa to Constantinople in 944 under Emperor Romanos I Lekapenos, joined with the commemoration of the Holy Martyr Diomedes of Tarsus (d. c. 298).

[^483]: The festive Troparion of the Holy Mandylion (*Пречистому образу Твоему покланяемся, Благий...*, Tone 2), professing Christ's voluntary ascent upon the Cross to deliver mankind from the bondage of the enemy.

[^484]: The ancient pre-Nikonian Kontakion of the Holy Mandylion (*Сшедый съ небесе за милосердіе милости...*, Tone 2), an archaic hymnographic witness preserved in 15th-century Ruthenian and South Slavic codices and Old Believer liturgical books, which was later replaced in post-Nikonian synodal editions by *Неизреченнаго и Божественнаго Твоего смотрѣнія...*

[^485]: Physical leaf p413 records the mid-August commemorations during the Dormition afterfeast: August 17: Holy Martyr Myron of Cyzicus (d. 250); August 18: Holy Martyrs Florus and Laurus of Illyria (2nd c.); August 19: Holy Martyr Andrew Stratelates and his 2,553 soldiers martyred in Cilicia under Maximian (c. 302).

[^486]: August 20 commemorates the Holy Prophet Samuel, the 15th and last Judge of Israel (11th c. BC), who anointed Saul and David as kings of Israel. The Kontakion (*Яко драгий и честный даръ...*, Tone 3) celebrates his childhood dedication at Shiloh and his prophetic ministry.

[^487]: August 21–22 commemorations: August 21: Holy Apostle Thaddaeus of the Seventy and Holy Martyr Bassa of Edessa with her children; August 22: Holy Martyr Agathonicus of Nicomedia and his companions (d. under Maximian).

[^488]: Physical leaf p414 records the Kontakion of the Holy Martyr Agathonicus (*Званіе благо стяжавъ, богомудре...*, Tone 4, Podoben: "Ликъ ангельскій").

[^489]: August 23 marks the Apodosis (Apodosis / *Отданіе*) of the Feast of the Dormition, coinciding with the feast of the Holy Martyr Lupus of Novae (d. c. 306, faithful servant of St. Demetrius of Thessalonica). Vespers and Matins repeat the full festive hymnography of August 15.

[^490]: Physical leaf p415 marks August 24: the Holy Hieromartyr Eutyches (1st–2nd c.), disciple of St. John the Theologian and fellow-laborer of the Holy Apostle Paul. His Kontakion (*Апостоломъ сопричастникъ...*, Tone 3) extols him as a star dispelling the dark night of idolatry.

[^491]: August 25 commemorates the Translation of the Relics of the Holy Apostle Bartholomew from Anastasiopolis to the island of Lipari (c. 508), joined with the commemoration of the Holy Apostle Titus.

[^492]: Physical leaf p416 details the combined office for the Holy Apostles Bartholomew and Titus. The Kontakion to Titus (*Павловъ явился еси способникъ...*, Tone 2) commends his collaboration with the Apostle Paul in Crete. Liturgy pericopes: Epistle Titus 1:1–4; Gospel Matthew 5:14–19 (lection 23).

[^493]: August 26 commemorates the Holy Martyrs Adrian and Natalia of Nicomedia (c. 306 under Emperor Galerius Maximian), celebrated with two Idiomela (Tone 1 and Tone 6), Troparion (*Мученицы Твои, Господи...*), and Kontakion (*Мучениковъ возсія божественная память...*, Tone 4).

[^494]: An early modern East Slavic epigraphic gloss in brown cursive script (*скоропись*) in the lower margin of folio 214r: *"Сего днь празноуеть праздникъ срѣтенію иконѣ пр[ечистые] вл[а]д[ы]ч[и]ца наша б[огороди]ца, иже гл[агол]ють володимерьская"* ("On this day is celebrated the feast of the Meeting of the Icon of our Most Pure Lady Theotokos, which is called Vladimirskaya"). This refers to the feast established in commemoration of the miraculous deliverance of Moscow from the invasion of Khan Tamerlane in 1395, officially instituted across Rus' liturgical calendars on August 26.

[^495]: Physical leaf p417 marks August 27: Our Venerable Father Poemen the Great (c. 340–450), renowned desert father of the Egyptian Skete whose sayings form a major foundation of the *Apophthegmata Patrum*. Kontakion: Tone 4, Podoben: "Явился еси".

[^496]: August 28 commemorates Our Venerable Father Moses the Black (the Ethiopian / *Муринъ*, c. 330–405), former brigand turned desert father and martyr in Scetis. The Kontakion (*Мурины в лице заушивъ...*, Tone 4, Podoben: "Явился еси") uses *Мурины* allegorically for the dark demonic forces vanquished through spiritual warfare.

[^497]: August 29 commemorates the Beheading of the Honorable Head of the Holy, Glorious Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John (*Усѣкновеніе честныя главы святаго славнаго Пророка, Предтечи и Крестителя Іоанна*), celebrated with an All-Night Vigil and strict day of fasting.

[^498]: Physical leaf p418 details the vigil order for the Beheading of St. John the Baptist, prescribing three Old Testament lections (Isaiah 40:1–3, 5, 9; 41:17–18; 45:8; 48:20–21; 54:1; Malachi 3:1–3, 5–7, 12, 18; 4:1–6; Wisdom 4:7, 11–14; 5:15–6:3) and the solemn festive Kontakion (*Предтечево славное усѣкновеніе...*, Tone 5).

[^499]: Physical leaf p419 concludes the services for the Beheading of the Forerunner with Liturgy pericopes: Epistle Acts 13:25–32 (lection 33); Gospel Mark 6:14–30 (lection 24).

[^500]: August 30 commemorates the Holy Patriarchs of Constantinople: Alexander (d. 337, champion of the Nicene Council), John the Cappadocian (d. 520), and Paul the New (d. 784, advocate of icon veneration).

[^501]: Physical leaf p420 preserves the additional Kontakion of the Forerunner (*Иродъ, Предтече, беззаконновавъ...*, Tone 4), chanted on August 30 during the commemoration with the Holy Patriarchs.

[^502]: August 31: The Deposition of the Precious Sash (Cincture / Belt / *Положеніе честнаго пояса*) of the Most Holy Theotokos in the Chalkoprateia church in Constantinople (*въ Халкопратіи*), established during the reign of Emperor Theodosius II and Empress Pulcheria (c. 5th c.) and later renewed under Emperor Leo VI. This feast concludes the Byzantine and Ruthenian liturgical year (Indiction).

[^503]: The Kontakion of the Deposition of the Sash of the Theotokos (*Честнаго пояса твоего положеніе...*, Tone 4, Podoben: "Явился еси") and Liturgy pericopes: Epistle Hebrews 9:1–7 (the sanctuary, golden censer, and ark of the covenant); Gospel Luke 10:38–42; 11:27–28.

[^504]: Conclusion of the Divine Liturgy lections for the feast of the Deposition of the Precious Sash of the Most Holy Theotokos (August 31), carrying over from physical leaf p420. The Epistle is Hebrews 9:1–7, Alleluia from Psalm 131:8, Gospel from Luke 10:38–42; 11:27–28, and Communion Hymn from Psalm 115:4.

[^505]: Opening landmark headpiece of the Pre-Lenten and Triodion cycle: "НД҃ЛѦ Ѡ МЫТАРИ И Ѡ фарисеи · въ слѣдованіе великаго · д҃ · десѧтнаго поста" (Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee; in the sequence of the Great Forty-day Fast). The numeral "д҃ · десѧтнаго" represents the Quadragesima (Четыредесятница).

[^506]: Typikon rubrics for the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee: Saturday Vespers with 10 stichera (combining the Octoechos in Tone 1 with Triodion idiomela), the transfer of the commemorated Menaion Saint to Compline, Matins with Polyeleos and Graduals of the tone, and Triodion canons.

[^507]: Tone 2 Kontakion of the Publican and the Pharisee: "Мытаря древле слезяще оправдалъ еси, Спасе благый, убѣсти возпивша, и лицемѣра помоли[вшася] презрѣвъ...". This reflects an ancient pre-Nikonian Byzantine-Slavic hymnographic witness preserved in 15th-century Ruthenian and South Slavic Triodia, distinct from the standard post-Nikonian text ("Фарисеева убежим высокоглаголания..."). The epigraphic witness in Fiol records "убѣсти" (crying out in confession/distress) and "лицемѣра" (the hypocrite).

[^508]: Liturgical order for the Hours and the Divine Liturgy on the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee, taking the Octoechos Beatitudes and the appointed readings of the Sunday.

[^509]: Polemical anti-Monophysite rubric regarding the fast-free week following the Sunday of the Publican and the Pharisee. The rubric specifically commands the consumption of cheese, eggs, and meat every day (including Wednesday and Friday) in order to refute and overturn the Armenian fast known historically as the "Artzivourion" (Арцивуриев пост, Artziburion), an ancient non-Chalcedonian pre-Lenten abstinence.

[^510]: Sunday of the Prodigal Son (Недѣля о блуднѣм сынѣ): Saturday Vespers order with 10 stichera (combining 3 Resurrectional, 3 Anatolian, and 2 Triodion idiomela doubled), and alphabetical stichera at the Aposticha.

[^511]: Sunday of the Prodigal Son Matins rubrics: Kathismata psalmody, Polyeleos, Graduals of the tone, and canons (Resurrectional on 4, Cross-Resurrectional on 4, Theotokion on 2, and Triodion on 6).

[^512]: Kontakion of the Prodigal Son in Tone 3. The 1491 Fiol print exhibits distinctive archaic Ruthenian phonetic/orthographic spellings: "Ѡтескыꙗ" for "Отеческия", "батытство" for "богатство", "блꙋнаго" for "блуднаго", and "пре тобою" for "пред Тобою".

[^513]: Meatfare Memorial Saturday (Суббота мясопустная): The universal commemoration of all departed Orthodox Christians from the ages, fathers and brethren.

[^514]: Liturgical order of Meatfare Saturday: Vespers replaces the daily Prokeimenon with the Tone 8 funeral Alleluia and Psalm verses (Ps. 64:5; 101:13); troparion "Who by depth of wisdom" (Глубиною мудрости); and dual Epistles (1 Cor. 6:12–20 for the day; 1 Thess. 4:13–17 for the reposed) and Gospels (Luke 21:8–9, 25–27, 33–36; John 5:24–30).

[^515]: Liturgical cross-reference noting that the identical ancestral funeral order is executed without change on the Saturday before Pentecost (Trinity Memorial Saturday).

[^516]: Meatfare Sunday (Недѣля мясопустная), dedicated to the commemoration of the Second Coming of Christ and the Last Judgment.

[^517]: Meatfare Sunday Matins rubrics: "God is the Lord", Resurrectional Troparion, Polyeleos, Graduals, and the 8-troparion Triodion canon.

[^518]: Tone 1 Automelon Kontakion of Meatfare Sunday: "Егда приидеши, Боже, на землю со славою...". In the 1491 Fiol recension, the clause "и тайная обличаются" ("and the hidden things are revealed") is omitted in the printed text, passing immediately from "книгы разгыбаются" to "тогда избави мя огня негасимаго".

[^519]: Divine Liturgy lections for Meatfare Sunday: Epistle from 1 Corinthians 8:8–9:2 (warning against causing a brother to stumble through eating food offered to idols) and Gospel from Matthew 25:31–46 (the Last Judgment and the separation of the sheep from the goats).

[^520]: Cheesefare Week (Сырная седмица / Масленица) liturgical regulations: Weekday Menaion services are subordinated and sung at Compline, while the Saturday and Sunday services are dedicated to the Triodion commemorations.

[^521]: Rubrical directive granting the ecclesiarch discretion regarding the transfer of Menaion commemorations on Cheesefare Saturday and Sunday.

[^522]: Cheesefare Saturday (Суббота сыропустная): The universal commemoration of all the Holy Venerable and God-bearing Fathers who shone forth in fasting and monastic asceticism.

[^523]: Cheesefare Saturday order for Vespers and Matins, including the Troparion to the Fathers ("O God of our fathers...") and the temple saint's canon combined with the Triodion canon on 8.

[^524]: Archaic Kontakion of the Holy Ascetic Fathers: "Яко благочестия проповедники и нечестия потребители...". Fiol 1491 preserves an archaic variant reading: "любоврачье снающе... въ смиренѣ съвръшенѣ" (knowing their loving cure... in perfect humility), documenting pre-Nikonian hymnographic phrasing.

[^525]: Divine Liturgy lections for Cheesefare Saturday: Epistle from Galatians 5:22–6:2 (the fruits of the Spirit) and Gospel from Matthew 11:27–30 ("Take My yoke upon you, and learn of Me; for I am meek and lowly in heart").

[^526]: Cheesefare Sunday (Недѣля сыропустная), also known as Forgiveness Sunday and the Commemoration of the Expulsion of Adam from Paradise.

[^527]: Cheesefare Sunday Matins order: Resurrectional Troparion, Kathismata, Polyeleos, Graduals, and the Triodion canon on 6.

[^528]: Tone 6 Kontakion of Cheesefare Sunday: "Премудрости наставниче, и смыслу давче...". Fiol 1491 witnesses "еже мудрости Слове" (O Word of wisdom) where later editions read "Отчее Слово" (Word of the Father), as well as the dual pronoun/noun construction "се бо устна ма доима не възбраню" (for lo, I will not refrain both my lips).

[^529]: Divine Liturgy lections for Cheesefare Sunday: Epistle from Romans 13:11–14:4 (putting on the armor of light and walking honestly as in the day) and Gospel from Matthew 6:14–21 (forgiving others their trespasses, fasting without hypocrisy, and laying up treasure in heaven).

[^530]: Historical rubrical statute regarding the Polyeleos: From Cheesefare Sunday until the Exaltation of the Holy Cross (September 14), the chanting of the Polyeleos ("Many-merciful") is omitted at Sunday Matins throughout the entire summer cycle, being reserved exclusively for Despotic (Dominical) Feasts.

[^531]: Cheesefare Sunday evening (Forgiveness Vespers): The solemn transition into Great Lent. The Great Prokeimenon in Tone 8 is chanted: "Turn not away Thy face from Thy servant, for I am in trouble; hear me speedily; attend unto my soul, and deliver it" (Ps. 68:18–19).

[^532]: The ancient Byzantine and Ruthenian monastic Rite of Mutual Forgiveness: The execution of Lenten metanias, the chanting of the penitential troparia, the Prayer of St. Ephrem the Syrian ("O Lord and Master of my life..."), and the mutual veneration of the holy icons with the forgiveness dialogue: "Остави мя на скондалѣтехъ по обычаю" (Forgive me wherein I have caused scandal/offense, according to custom).

[^533]: Major section headpiece marking the formal beginning of Great Lent: "НАЧАЛО СТ҃ГО ВЕЛИКАГО · Д҃ · РЕДЕСѦТНАГО ПОСТА" (Beginning of the Holy Great Forty-Day Fast). The numerical abbreviation "Д҃ · РЕДЕСѦТНАГО" represents the Quadragesima (Четыредесятница). Fiol 1491 here establishes the distinctive order of the Lenten weekday cursus (liturgical Hours, Great Compline, and Midnight Office).

[^534]: Typikon order for Clean Monday (Чистый понедельник): Matins chanted with Alleluia and the Hymns to the Trinity (Троичны) in the tone of the week, the psalmody of the Psalter Kathismata intercalated with Triodion and Menaion sessional hymns, and the execution of the First, Third, Sixth, and Ninth Hours with Kathisma psalmody and Lenten prostrations.

[^535]: Ancient Studite and Ruthenian liturgical statute regarding the opening days of Great Lent: "Подобаеть вѣдати · ꙗко не приꙗхомъ стварѧти преждесщ҃еныꙗ до среды · понеже по преданію ст҃ыхъ ѡц҃ъ постатѧсѧ браꙗ всѧ до среды · могоущіи же до пѧтка" (It is fitting to know that we have not received the tradition to celebrate the Presanctified before Wednesday, because according to the tradition of the Holy Fathers all the brethren fast until Wednesday, and those who are able, until Friday). This documents the rigorous monastic total fast (асития / сухоядение) during Clean Week.

[^536]: Sacerdotal and diaconal dialogue at the Presanctified Liturgy: The exclamation "Келепсаите" represents an exact phonetic Church Slavonic transcription of the Byzantine Greek "Κελεύσατε" (*Keleusate* — "Command!" / "Bid, reverend father!"), addressed by the deacon to the celebrant before the blessing with the candle and censer ("The Light of Christ illumines all").

[^537]: Monastic ceremonial directive ordering the candle-lighter (*kandilaptes* / кандиловозжигатель) to strike the iron semantron (*klepalo* / било): "кандилаптие же знаменаеть · в желѣзное" during the Old Testament reading from Proverbs, signaling the impending solemn chanting of "Let my prayer be set forth" (Да исправится молитва моя) and the prostrations of the brethren.

[^538]: Sacerdotal rubric governing the covering of the Presanctified Gifts after the Great Entrance: the priest removes both veils (*sudaria*) and covers both the diskos and the chalice together with a single large veil or with the aer (*vozdukh*).

[^539]: The consecrated Eucharistic Lamb is designated as the "Holy Artos" (ст҃го артоса / арьтоса, from the Greek ἄрτος, *artos* — the consecrated Bread/Host) at the ritual Elevation before communion: "The Presanctified Holy Things unto the holy!"

[^540]: Archaic Ruthenian terminology for the post-communion ablution: "и покꙋшнивають водици" (*i pokushnivayut voditsi* — "and they partake of the warm water"), describing the purification rinse taken by the communicants after receiving the Holy Mysteries.

[^541]: Monastic dietary rule (типикон о трапезѣ) for Clean Week: dry food (сухоядение / xerophagy) is taken on Wednesday following the Presanctified Liturgy; on Thursday no meal is set; and dry food is taken again on Friday afternoon.

[^542]: Rubrical transition marking the cessation of Lenten weekday penitential prostrations at Friday Vespers ("на вечерни упражнѧютсѧ поклоны"), as Friday evening initiates the festal rest of the Sabbath (St. Theodore Saturday).

[^543]: Order of the Liturgy of the Presanctified Gifts on Friday of the First Week of Great Lent, combining the Lenten lections with the proper stichera and commemorations of the Great-Martyr Theodore the Recruit (Theodore Tyro) and the blessing of the Kolyvo.

[^544]: Universal liturgical statute for all Fridays of Great Lent: Great Compline is chanted in its entirety without prostrations, incorporating the canon for the reposed from the Octoechos in the current tone in preparation for the ancestral Memorial Saturday.

[^545]: Tone 2 Troparion of the Great-Martyr Theodore the Recruit: "Веліа вѣрѣ исправленіа..." ("Great are the achievements of faith..."). Fiol 1491 records the archaic witness: "ѡгне же гьсѧ · ꙗко хлѣбъ сладокъ тр҃ци принѣсѧ" (having been consumed by fire, as sweet bread he was offered unto the Trinity).

[^546]: Matins order for the First Saturday of Great Lent (St. Theodore Saturday): Kathisma 16 and Kathisma 17 (*Amomos*, Ps. 118: "Blessed are the undefiled") chanted with Alleluia, and the canons of the temple saint and St. Theodore chanted on 6 each.

[^547]: Tone 8 Kontakion of Saint Theodore the Recruit: "Вѣру Христову яко щитъ внутрь пріемъ въ сердцы..." ("Having received the faith of Christ within thy heart as a shield...").

[^548]: Monastic dietary dispensation for St. Theodore Saturday: consumption of boiled greens/vegetables (*ovara obvarena*), white and black olives, oil and warm food, kolyvo, and wine measured by the *krasovolion* (красоволя, the customary monastic cup).

[^549]: Monastic canon of the Holy Forty Days regarding fish consumption: "ꙗко въ ст҃жа · м҃ · в҃ · приꙗхомъ ꙗсти рыбы · в праздникы бл҃говѣщеніа · и в н҃елю цвѣтнꙋю" (for in the Holy Forty Days we have received the rule to eat fish twice: on the feast of the Annunciation, and on Palm Sunday).
